## People

### Ada Lovelace
- `ada-lovelace` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^ada-lovelace
- what: English mathematician (1815-1852) whose 1843 notes on Babbage's Analytical Engine hold the first published program and the first argument that machines cannot originate anything
- aka: Augusta Ada King, Countess of Lovelace
- receipts: translated Luigi Menabrea's paper on the Analytical Engine and added seven notes (A-G), published September 1843 in Taylor's Scientific Memoirs under the initials 'AAL' (1843-09) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ada_Lovelace [medium]; Note G gives a method for computing Bernoulli numbers, often called the first published computer program (1843-09) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ada_Lovelace [medium]; Alan Turing answered her in 1950 as 'Lady Lovelace's Objection' in 'Computing Machinery and Intelligence' (1950-10) https://academic.oup.com/mind/article/LIX/236/433/986238 [high]; the US Department of Defense named its Ada programming language after her; the reference manual was approved on her birthday, 1980-12-10 (1980-12-10) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ada_Lovelace [medium]; Ada Lovelace Day, the second Tuesday of October since 2009, honors women in STEM (2009) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ada_Lovelace [medium]
- quotes: "The Analytical Engine has no pretensions whatever to originate anything." (Ada Lovelace, Notes on Menabrea's 'Sketch of the Analytical Engine', Note G (source has 'originate' in italics), 1843-09) https://www.fourmilab.ch/babbage/sketch.html; "compose elaborate and scientific pieces of music of any degree of complexity or extent" (Ada Lovelace, Notes on Menabrea's 'Sketch of the Analytical Engine', Note A (on what the engine might do), 1843-09) https://www.fourmilab.ch/babbage/sketch.html
- name play: **Lovelace** love-lace: love plus lace, and the Jacquard loom's punched lace cards that fed the Engine; a love song and a programming manual in one surname; **Ada** a palindrome, the same read forward and back like a loop; also a programming language; **poetical science** her own phrase for her method; the channel's whole genre in two words
- pivots (jep drafts): "She said the engine could write music. Here we are." (her 1843 note + an AI music video) / "No pretensions to originate anything, just like this song" (the Lovelace objection + self-aware AI music) / "Lace the cards, weave the code, love the machine" (Jacquard punch cards + her name + a love song)
- visuals: a Jacquard punched card; a brass Difference Engine gear column; a Victorian table of Bernoulli numbers (Note G's diagram); a quill writing beside a pile of punched cards
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^algorithm|Algorithm]] Note G is the first published algorithm for a machine; [[kit-lexicon#^creator|Creator / Creation]] her objection asks whether a machine can create or only follow; [[kit-lexicon#^score|Score]] she imagined the engine composing music; [[kit-lexicon#^loop|Loop]] Note G's method repeats a cycle of operations; [[kit-lexicon#^turing-test|Turing Test / Imitation Game]] Turing's 1950 paper answers her by name
- links: [[#^alan-turing|Alan Turing]] (inspired), [[#^john-von-neumann|John von Neumann]] (same-family), [[#^suno|Suno]] (inspired)
- linked from: [[#^alan-turing|Alan Turing]], [[#^john-von-neumann|John von Neumann]]
- forms (counted): Lovelace, Ada Lovelace, Analytical Engine, Babbage | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0
- stakes mind, heart | registers tender, awe
- risk: historians dispute how much of the program was hers versus Babbage's; say 'published' and 'her notes', avoid 'invented programming'
- note: She is the only figure in the atlas who predicted machine-composed music in writing, 183 years before an AI music video about AI. Open or close a song on her.

### Alan Turing
- `alan-turing` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^alan-turing
- what: British mathematician (1912-1954) who defined computation in 1936, helped break Enigma at Bletchley Park, and proposed the imitation game, later called the Turing test, in 1950
- aka: the father of computer science
- receipts: 'On Computable Numbers' (Proceedings of the London Mathematical Society, 1936) defined the universal machine behind every computer (1936) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Turing [medium]; designed the bombe that broke Enigma traffic at Bletchley Park; the first bombe was installed 1940-03-18 (1940-03-18) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Turing [medium]; published 'Computing Machinery and Intelligence' in Mind, October 1950, proposing the imitation game (1950-10) https://academic.oup.com/mind/article/LIX/236/433/986238 [high]; prosecuted for gross indecency in 1952 and given chemical castration as a condition of probation; died 1954-06-07, ruled suicide; royal pardon 2013, and the 2017 'Alan Turing law' pardoned others (2013-12-24) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Turing [medium]; the Bank of England issued the Turing £50 note on his birthday, 2021-06-23 (2021-06-23) https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/news/2021/march/the-new-50-note-unveiled [high]
- quotes: "I propose to consider the question, 'Can machines think?'" (Alan Turing, opening of 'Computing Machinery and Intelligence', Mind LIX (236), 1950-10) https://academic.oup.com/mind/article/LIX/236/433/986238; "Nevertheless I believe that at the end of the century the use of words and general educated opinion will have altered" (Alan Turing, same paper, section 6, 1950-10) https://academic.oup.com/mind/article/LIX/236/433/986238
- name play: **Turing / touring** homophone: a band on tour, a machine touring the world; **Turing / turning** one letter off 'turning': the turning point, the turn of the tape head; **the imitation game** his own name for the test doubles as the definition of a cover song
- pivots (jep drafts): "Can machines think? Ask me after the show" (Turing's 1950 question + a performer's tease) / "I passed your test by playing someone else" (the imitation game + a closeted life + an AI persona) / "Fifty pounds for his face, a conviction for his life" (the 2021 note + the 1952 conviction)
- visuals: an Enigma machine with its rotors out; a wall of spinning bombe drums at Bletchley Park; a paper tape with a read head and a single symbol; a red £50 note held to the light
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^turing-test|Turing Test / Imitation Game]] he proposed it as the imitation game; [[kit-lexicon#^halt|Halt (halting problem)]] his 1936 paper proves no machine can decide halting in general; [[kit-lexicon#^decode|Encode / Decode]] Bletchley's codebreaking is decoding in the literal sense; [[kit-lexicon#^persona|Persona]] the imitation game is a test of a persona; [[kit-lexicon#^algorithm|Algorithm]] his machine is the formal definition of an algorithm
- links: [[#^turing-award|Turing Award]] (inspired), [[#^john-searle|John Searle]] (rival), [[#^ada-lovelace|Ada Lovelace]] (same-family), [[#^john-von-neumann|John von Neumann]] (same-family), [[#^ex-machina|Ex Machina (2014)]] (inspired)
- linked from: [[#^ada-lovelace|Ada Lovelace]], [[#^colossus|Colossus: The Forbin Project]], [[#^dartmouth-1956|Dartmouth workshop 1956]], [[#^ex-machina|Ex Machina (2014)]], [[#^john-searle|John Searle]], [[#^john-von-neumann|John von Neumann]], [[#^ray-kurzweil|Ray Kurzweil]], [[#^thinking-machines|Thinking Machines Lab]], [[#^turing-award|Turing Award]], [[#^uk-aisi|UK AI Security Institute]]
- forms (counted): Turing, Turing test, imitation game, Enigma, Bletchley | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0
- stakes mind, heart | registers sad, awe
- risk: likeness and taste: his prosecution and death are real tragedy; avoid using them as a punchline. The death was ruled suicide and is debated by some biographers; say 'ruled suicide'
- note: Every AI chorus about passing for human is already his: the imitation game is the cleanest double reading in the field, a test for machines and a life lived under cover.

### Alexandr Wang
- `alexandr-wang` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^alexandr-wang
- what: Founder of Scale AI, the data-labeling company behind much of AI's training data, who became Meta's first Chief AI Officer after Meta bought 49% of Scale in June 2025
- aka: Alex Wang
- receipts: left MIT to co-found Scale AI in 2016 at about 19; became the youngest self-made billionaire in 2021 at 24 (2021) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandr_Wang [medium]; the Washington Post reported on 2023-08-28 on Scale's Remotasks contractors overseas under the headline 'Behind the AI boom, an army of overseas workers in digital sweatshops' (2023-08-28) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandr_Wang [medium]; ran a full-page Washington Post ad the day after the 2025 inauguration: 'Dear President Trump, America must win the AI war' (2025-01-21) https://techcrunch.com/2025/01/21/scale-ais-alexandr-wang-has-published-an-open-letter-lobbying-trump-to-invest-in-ai [high]; in June 2025 Meta invested about $14.3 billion for a non-voting 49% of Scale (valuing it at $29 billion) and hired Wang to lead Meta Superintelligence Labs as its first Chief AI Officer (2025-06-12) https://www.nasdaq.com/articles/meta-invests-143-bln-scale-ai-taps-ceo-alexandr-wang-lead-superintelligence-push [high]; Scale co-built the 'Humanity's Last Exam' benchmark with the Center for AI Safety, released January 2025 (2025-01) https://lastexam.ai/ [medium]
- quotes: "Dear President Trump, America must win the AI war" (Alexandr Wang, full-page Washington Post ad (reported by Semafor), 2025-01-21) https://www.semafor.com/article/01/20/2025/scale-ai-ceo-alexandr-wang-to-trump-america-must-win-the-ai-war
- name play: **Scale** his company's name is the AI field's master verb (scale up), a weighing scale (judging data), and a musical scale; **Alexandr** the dropped 'e' makes the name look compressed, like a token that saved a byte; **Wang** one syllable that rhymes with 'gang', 'bang', 'rang': an easy end-rhyme for a hook
- pivots (jep drafts): "Every smart machine was taught by hands you never paid" (data-labeling labor + any apprentice story) / "He sold the scale, then the scale weighed him in billions" (Scale AI's sale to Meta + the weighing scale) / "Run the scales till the music learns to sing" (musical scales + scaling laws)
- visuals: a full-page newspaper ad in bold serif: 'America must win the AI war'; rows of bounding boxes drawn around cars and stop signs; a laptop at a kitchen table with a task counter; a balance scale with data on one pan
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^label|Label]] Scale's product is labels; [[kit-lexicon#^ground-truth|Ground Truth]] labelers write the ground truth models learn from; [[kit-lexicon#^scaling|Scale / Scaling (laws)]] the company is named for the field's master verb; [[kit-lexicon#^race|Race / Arms Race]] his ad framed AI as a war with China; [[kit-lexicon#^asi|ASI / Superintelligence]] he runs Meta's 'superintelligence' lab; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] Humanity's Last Exam came out of Scale and CAIS
- links: [[#^scale-ai|Scale AI]] (founded), [[#^meta|Meta]] (works-at), [[#^mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]] (works-at), [[#^humanitys-last-exam|Humanity's Last Exam]] (made), [[#^china|China]] (rival)
- linked from: [[#^cais|Center for AI Safety (CAIS)]], [[#^humanitys-last-exam|Humanity's Last Exam]], [[#^mark-zuckerberg|Mark Zuckerberg]], [[#^meta|Meta]], [[#^scale-ai|Scale AI]]
- forms (counted): Alexandr Wang, Scale AI, Remotasks | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0
- stakes jobs, wallet, state | registers defiant, ominous

### Andrej Karpathy
- `andrej-karpathy` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^andrej-karpathy
- what: Slovak-born Canadian AI researcher, OpenAI founding member and former Tesla director of AI, who now runs the education startup Eureka Labs and coined 'vibe coding'
- aka: karpathy, the explainer
- receipts: listed among OpenAI's founding members in the launch post of 2015-12-11 (2015-12-11) https://openai.com/index/introducing-openai/ [high]; published 'Software 2.0' on 2017-11-11, arguing that neural network weights are the new source code (2017-11-11) https://karpathy.medium.com/software-2-0-a64152b37c35 [high]; led AI for Tesla's Autopilot as director of AI before leaving in 2022 (2022) https://venturebeat.com/ai/ex-openai-and-tesla-engineer-andrej-karpathy-announces-ai-native-school-eureka-labs [medium]; announced Eureka Labs, 'a new kind of school that is AI native', with first course LLM101n, on 2024-07-16 (2024-07-16) https://venturebeat.com/ai/ex-openai-and-tesla-engineer-andrej-karpathy-announces-ai-native-school-eureka-labs [high]; coined 'vibe coding' in a post on 2025-02-02; the term spread to dictionaries and a startup category within the year (2025-02-02) https://listserv.linguistlist.org/pipermail/ads-l/2025-March/166588.html [high]; told Dwarkesh Patel on 2025-10-17 that working agents will take about a decade and called much industry output 'slop' (2025-10-17) https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/andrej-karpathy [high]
- quotes: "The hottest new programming language is English" (Andrej Karpathy, post on Twitter (logged by Quote Investigator), 2023-01-24) https://quoteinvestigator.com/2024/10/20/hottest-program/; "There's a new kind of coding I call "vibe coding", where you fully give in to the vibes, embrace exponentials, and forget that the code even exists." (Andrej Karpathy, post on X (archived on the ADS-L list), 2025-02-02) https://listserv.linguistlist.org/pipermail/ads-l/2025-March/166588.html; "I feel like the industry is making too big of a jump and is trying to pretend like this is amazing, and it's not. It's slop." (Andrej Karpathy, Dwarkesh Podcast transcript, 2025-10-17) https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/andrej-karpathy
- name play: **Karpathy / car path** the surname splits into 'car path': the man who taught a car its path at Tesla; **vibe coding** his coinage turns code into a mood; every 'vibe' in a lyric points back at him; **Software 2.0** a version number as a manifesto; easy to bump to 3.0 in a lyric
- pivots (jep drafts): "Forget the code exists, just ride the vibe" (his vibe coding post + a party instruction) / "The hottest new language is the one you already speak" (prompting in English + a love song) / "He taught the car its path, then went back to school" (Tesla Autopilot then Eureka Labs + a career told as a road trip)
- visuals: a whiteboard with a hand-drawn tiny autograd graph; a car camera view with bounding boxes; a laptop with code scrolling past unread while someone nods to music; a chalkboard reading 'LLM101n'
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^vibe|Vibe (vibe coding)]] he coined vibe coding; [[kit-lexicon#^slop|Slop]] his 'It's slop' line about agents became a headline; [[kit-lexicon#^agent|Agent]] he said working agents will take about a decade; [[kit-lexicon#^weights|Weights]] Software 2.0 says the program now lives in the weights; [[kit-lexicon#^prompt|Prompt]] 'the hottest new programming language is English' is the prompt thesis in one line; [[kit-lexicon#^teacher-student|Teacher / Student (models)]] Eureka Labs pairs human course design with AI teachers
- links: [[#^openai|OpenAI]] (founded), [[#^tesla|Tesla]] (works-at), [[#^fei-fei-li|Fei-Fei Li]] (same-family), [[#^anysphere|Anysphere (Cursor)]] (inspired)

### Chris Olah
- `chris-olah` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^chris-olah
- what: Anthropic co-founder who leads its interpretability research, known for the 'circuits' picture of neural networks and for doing it all without an undergraduate degree
- aka: colah
- receipts: left the University of Toronto after about a year, and in 2012 took a $100,000 Thiel Fellowship (2012) https://80000hours.org/podcast/episodes/chris-olah-unconventional-career-path/ [high]; lead author of 'Zoom In: An Introduction to Circuits' (Distill, 2020-03-10), which argued networks can be read as features wired into circuits (2020-03-10) https://distill.pub/2020/circuits/zoom-in/ [high]; Anthropic's 'Scaling Monosemanticity' (May 2024) pulled up to 34 million features out of Claude 3 Sonnet; clamping a Golden Gate Bridge feature made 'Golden Gate Claude', public for a day on 2024-05-23 (2024-05-21) https://transformer-circuits.pub/2024/scaling-monosemanticity/ [high]; sat with Pope Leo XIV at the presentation of the Pope's first encyclical, on the dangers of AI, in late May 2026, and argued outsiders should oversee the industry (2026-06-03) https://fortune.com/2026/06/03/who-chris-olah-anthropic-cofounder-atheist-pope-leo-vatican-ai/ [high]
- quotes: "Some might believe that matters of AI are best handled by computer scientists like myself." ### Emily M. Bender
- `emily-bender` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^emily-bender
- what: University of Washington linguist who co-wrote 'On the Dangers of Stochastic Parrots' (2021) and 'The AI Con' (2025), the sharpest academic critic of language-model hype
- aka: the Bender Rule
- receipts: on the University of Washington linguistics faculty since 2003; directs its Computational Linguistics Laboratory (2003) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_M._Bender [medium]; co-wrote 'Climbing towards NLU' with Alexander Koller (ACL 2020), the 'octopus' argument that a model trained only on form cannot learn meaning (2020-07) https://aclanthology.org/2020.acl-main.463/ [high]; lead author of 'On the Dangers of Stochastic Parrots: Can Language Models Be Too Big?', presented at ACM FAccT in March 2021 with Timnit Gebru, Angelina McMillan-Major and 'Shmargaret Shmitchell' (2021-03-03) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3442188.3445922 [high]; the 'Bender Rule': always name the language you are working with (2019) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_M._Bender [medium]; published 'The AI Con' with sociologist Alex Hanna (Harper, 2025) (2025) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_M._Bender [medium]
- quotes: "an LM is a system for haphazardly stitching together sequences of linguistic forms it has observed in its vast training data, according to probabilistic information about how they combine, but without any reference to meaning: a stochastic parrot." (Emily M. Bender, Timnit Gebru, Angelina McMillan-Major, Shmargaret Shmitchell, 'On the Dangers of Stochastic Parrots', FAccT '21, section 6, 2021-03-03) https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3442188.3445922
- name play: **Bender** a binge, a metal-bender, and Futurama's lying, thieving robot: the anti-hype scholar shares a name with TV's most famous misbehaving machine; **the Bender Rule** sounds like one of Asimov's laws of robotics, and it is a rule for the humans
- pivots (jep drafts): "A trillion feathers and the parrot still don't know the word" (the stochastic parrot + the scale argument) / "Name the language, she said, every time you speak of it" (the Bender Rule + a demand for precise words about AI) / "The octopus on the cable learned our words, never our island" (her 2020 thought experiment + a chatbot reading the internet)
- visuals: a parrot perched on a blinking server rack; an octopus wrapped round an undersea cable, listening; a carnival barker's stage with a curtain over a machine
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^stochastic-parrot|Stochastic Parrot]] she coined the term; [[kit-lexicon#^hype|Hype]] her podcast and book target AI hype directly; [[kit-lexicon#^next-token|Next-Token Prediction]] 'stitching together sequences' by probability is next-token prediction; [[kit-lexicon#^sampling|Sample / Sampling]] the 'stochastic' in the parrot is sampling
- links: [[#^timnit-gebru|Timnit Gebru]] (same-family), [[#^gary-marcus|Gary Marcus]] (same-family)
- linked from: [[#^john-searle|John Searle]], [[#^timnit-gebru|Timnit Gebru]]

### Fei-Fei Li
- `fei-fei-li` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^fei-fei-li
- what: Stanford computer scientist who built ImageNet, co-founded Stanford's Institute for Human-Centered AI and founded World Labs, a spatial-intelligence startup AMD agreed to buy in 2026
- aka: godmother of AI, Li Fei-Fei
- receipts: the ImageNet paper (CVPR 2009) launched a database of 3.2 million labeled images in 5,247 categories, the set AlexNet broke open in 2012 (2009-06-20) https://www.image-net.org/static_files/papers/imagenet_cvpr09.pdf [high]; founding co-director of Stanford HAI (Human-Centered AI), launched March 2019 (2019-03-18) https://profiles.stanford.edu/fei-fei-li [high]; published the memoir 'The Worlds I See: Curiosity, Exploration, and Discovery at the Dawn of AI', November 2023 (2023-11-07) https://profiles.stanford.edu/fei-fei-li [high]; World Labs released Marble, a world model that turns text or images into editable 3D worlds, to the public on 2025-11-12 (2025-11-12) https://www.worldlabs.ai/blog [high]; AMD agreed on 2026-09-28 to buy World Labs in an all-stock deal worth about $8.2 billion; Li to become AMD's executive vice president and chief scientist, reporting to Lisa Su; close expected by end of 2026 pending regulators (2026-09-28) https://techcrunch.com/2026/09/28/amd-will-acquire-fei-fei-lis-world-labs-for-8-2-billion/ [high]
- quotes: "Yet they remain wordsmiths in the dark; eloquent but inexperienced, knowledgeable but ungrounded." (Fei-Fei Li, essay 'From Words to Worlds' on her Substack, about large language models, 2025-11-10) https://drfeifei.substack.com/p/from-words-to-worlds-spatial-intelligence
- name play: **Fei-Fei** a doubled syllable: an echo, a loop, a name that repeats itself like a training epoch; **Li / see** Li rhymes with 'see'; her memoir is 'The Worlds I See' and her life's work is teaching machines to see; **godmother** the press title reads as fairy godmother: she waved a dataset and the pumpkin became deep learning; **ImageNet** a net that catches images: a fishing net dragged through the internet
- pivots (jep drafts): "Wordsmiths in the dark, eloquent, and never once inside a room" (her essay on language models + any chatbot describing a world it never touched) / "She labeled the world so a machine could open its eyes" (ImageNet and the 2012 vision breakthrough + a parent naming things for a child) / "The godmother sold the castle to the chip queen" (World Labs bought by AMD under Lisa Su + a fairy tale ending)
- visuals: a wall-sized mosaic of tiny labeled thumbnails (cat, dog, mushroom) like the ImageNet grid; a 3D world built from glowing splats, rotating in a browser window (Marble); AMD's black-and-red logo over a Stanford cardinal-red banner
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^label|Label]] ImageNet is the canonical hand-labeled dataset; [[kit-lexicon#^ground-truth|Ground Truth]] her crowd-sourced labels became vision's ground truth; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] the ImageNet challenge was the benchmark deep learning broke in 2012; [[kit-lexicon#^pixel|Pixel(s)]] her field begins at the pixel and ends at the world; [[kit-lexicon#^render|Render]] Marble renders explorable worlds from a prompt; [[kit-lexicon#^environment|Environment / Gym]] World Labs builds environments that train robots

### Garry Kasparov
- `garry-kasparov` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^garry-kasparov
- what: Russian-born chess world champion (1985-2000) who lost to IBM's Deep Blue in 1997, accused IBM of foul play, and later argued for human-machine 'centaur' teams
- aka: Garry
- receipts: became the youngest undisputed world champion at 22 on 1985-11-09, beating Anatoly Karpov 13-11 (1985-11-09) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garry_Kasparov [medium]; beat Deep Blue 4-2 in Philadelphia in February 1996, then lost the New York rematch 2.5-3.5 on 1997-05-11 after resigning game 6 in under twenty moves (1997-05-11) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Blue_versus_Garry_Kasparov [medium]; after game 2 (1997-05-04) accused IBM of cheating, suspecting human help behind 37.Be4, a move he found too subtle for a computer (1997-05-04) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Blue_versus_Garry_Kasparov [medium]; played the first 'advanced chess' match (human plus computer per side) against Veselin Topalov in León, Spain, June 1998, drawn 3-3 (1998-06) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_chess [medium]; published 'Deep Thinking: Where Machine Intelligence Ends and Human Creativity Begins' (PublicAffairs, 2017) (2017) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garry_Kasparov [medium]; Russia designated him a 'foreign agent' in 2022 for his opposition to Putin's government (2022) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garry_Kasparov [medium]
- quotes: "Weak human + machine + better process was superior to a strong computer alone" (Garry Kasparov, 'The Chess Master and the Computer', The New York Review of Books, 2010-02-11) https://www.nybooks.com/articles/2010/02/11/the-chess-master-and-the-computer/
- name play: **Deep Blue** the machine's name reads as sadness (blue) and depth (deep): the first AI that made a champion sad on TV; IBM is 'Big Blue'; **Kasparov** 'Kaspar' (Caspar), one of the three kings who followed a star: a king of the board brought low by a machine; **centaur** his half-human, half-machine teams give the myth a job
- pivots (jep drafts): "Too human to be a machine, he said, about the machine" (his 1997 cheating accusation + every 'it can't be AI' reaction since) / "Deep blue: the color of the move that beat you" (IBM's Deep Blue + sadness) / "Weak human, good machine, better process: the centaur wins" (his NYRB line + a copilot ad)
- visuals: a chess clock and a resigned king lying on its side; a black IBM cabinet tower with 'Deep Blue' on the side; a centaur chess piece; a man with his head in his hands under a 1997 broadcast banner
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^copilot|Copilot / Autopilot]] his centaur idea is the copilot idea, twenty years early; [[kit-lexicon#^human-in-the-loop|Human in the Loop]] advanced chess is a human-in-the-loop format; [[kit-lexicon#^black-box|Black Box]] he demanded IBM's logs because the box would not explain itself; [[kit-lexicon#^deception|Deception]] his first reaction to a strong machine move was to suspect a hidden human; [[kit-lexicon#^compute|Compute]] Deep Blue won by brute-force search on custom chips

### Gary Marcus

- what: Cognitive scientist, NYU professor emeritus and author, the best-known critic of the claim that scaling deep learning alone will reach general intelligence
- aka: Marcus on AI

- quotes: "Deep learning, which is fundamentally a technique for recognizing patterns, is at its best when all we need are rough-ready results, where stakes are low and perfect results optional." ### Geoffrey Hinton
- `geoffrey-hinton` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^geoffrey-hinton
- what: British-Canadian computer scientist, co-inventor of the Boltzmann machine and champion of backpropagation, who left Google in 2023 to warn about the field he helped build
- aka: Geoff Hinton, the Godfather of AI
- receipts: AlexNet (Krizhevsky, Sutskever, Hinton) won ImageNet 2012 with 15.3% top-5 error against 26.2% for the runner-up, the moment deep learning took over (2012-12-03) https://papers.nips.cc/paper/4824-imagenet-classification-with-deep-convolutional-neural-networks [high]; auctioned his three-person startup DNNresearch in a Lake Tahoe hotel in December 2012; he stopped the bidding at $44 million and sold to Google (2012-12-01) https://www.wired.com/story/secret-auction-race-ai-supremacy-google-microsoft-baidu/ [medium]; shared the 2018 ACM Turing Award, announced 2019-03-27, with Yann LeCun and Yoshua Bengio for deep learning (2019-03-27) https://www.acm.org/articles/bulletins/2019/march/turing-award-2018 [high]; announced on 2023-05-01 that he had left Google after a decade so he could speak freely about AI risk (2023-05-01) https://www.cbsnews.com/news/artificial-intelligence-geoffrey-hinton-leaves-google-dangers-of-ai/ [high]; won the 2024 Nobel Prize in Physics with John Hopfield 'for foundational discoveries and inventions that enable machine learning with artificial neural networks' (2024-10-08) https://www.nobelprize.org/prizes/physics/2024/press-release/ [high]; told BBC Radio 4 in December 2024 he puts the chance of AI wiping out humanity within 30 years at 10 to 20 percent (2024-12-27) https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2024/dec/27/godfather-of-ai-raises-odds-of-the-technology-wiping-out-humanity-over-next-30-years [high]
- quotes: "I console myself with the normal excuse: If I hadn't done it, somebody else would have." (Geoffrey Hinton, interview with Cade Metz, The New York Times (quoted by Fortune), 2023-05-01) https://fortune.com/2023/05/01/godfather-ai-geoffrey-hinton-quit-google-regrets-lifes-work-bad-actors/; "Actually, I left so that I could talk about the dangers of AI without considering how this impacts Google." (Geoffrey Hinton, post on Twitter, replying to the NYT story (reported by MobileSyrup), 2023-05-01) https://mobilesyrup.com/2023/05/01/google-ai-veteran-geoffrey-hinton-leaves-company-to-warn-of-ai-risks/
- name play: **Godfather** the press title reads as mafia: the Godfather walks away from the family business and starts talking; **Hint-on** the surname splits into 'hint on': the man who switched the warning light on; **back-prop** his signature method sends the error backwards; he spent his late career walking his own work back
- pivots (jep drafts): "The godfather left the family so he could talk" (Hinton leaving Google in 2023 + a mob boss turning witness) / "I sent the error backwards, now I carry it myself" (backpropagation + a creator's regret) / "Ten to twenty percent, he said it like the weather" (his p(doom) on BBC radio + a forecast for rain)
- visuals: a Nobel medal next to a cardboard box of desk things on the way out; two consumer GPUs glowing in a 2012 tower case; a casino hotel auction paddle reading $44M; a Google badge left on an empty desk

### John Searle
- `john-searle` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^john-searle
- what: American philosopher (1932-2025) at UC Berkeley whose 1980 Chinese Room argument claims that running a program is not the same as understanding
- aka: the man in the room
- receipts: published 'Minds, Brains, and Programs' in Behavioral and Brain Sciences, 1980, introducing the Chinese Room against 'strong AI' (1980) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Searle [medium]; received the US National Humanities Medal in 2004 (2004) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Searle [medium]; UC Berkeley revoked his emeritus status in June 2019 after finding he sexually harassed and retaliated against a former student and employee (2019-06) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Searle [medium]; died on 2025-09-17 at 93, three years into the chatbot era his argument is now used to judge (2025-09-17) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Searle [medium]
- name play: **Searle / surly** sound-alike: the surly man in the room who will not be impressed; **the Room** his argument is a place: a locked room with a rulebook, a slot in, a slot out; **Searle / search** one letter from 'search': looking up symbols without knowing what they mean
- pivots (jep drafts): "I pass you perfect answers through a slot I cannot read" (the Chinese Room + a chatbot's reply + a shy lover) / "The rulebook speaks, the man inside just turns the page" (Searle's argument + a lip-sync performance) / "Syntax all night long and still no meaning in the morning" (syntax versus semantics + a hollow romance)
- visuals: a closed room with a mail slot and slips of paper in Chinese characters; a thick rulebook open on a desk; a man reading by lamplight, back to the camera; two paper slips: one in, one out
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^chinese-room|Chinese Room]] his argument; [[kit-lexicon#^consciousness|Consciousness]] he held that understanding needs the brain's causal powers; [[kit-lexicon#^turing-test|Turing Test / Imitation Game]] the Room is built to show passing the test proves nothing; [[kit-lexicon#^stochastic-parrot|Stochastic Parrot]] the 2021 parrot critique is a statistical cousin of his room; [[kit-lexicon#^black-box|Black Box]] the room is a black box whose outputs look like understanding

### John von Neumann
- `john-von-neumann` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^john-von-neumann
- what: Hungarian-American mathematician (1903-1957) behind the stored-program computer design, game theory and self-reproducing automata, and the first person on record discussing a technological 'singularity'
- aka: Johnny, Neumann János
- receipts: wrote the 'First Draft of a Report on the EDVAC' (dated 1945-06-30), the stored-program design now called the von Neumann architecture, with circuit elements modeled on McCulloch-Pitts neurons; it left co-designers Eckert and Mauchly off the authorship (1945-06-30) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Draft_of_a_Report_on_the_EDVAC [medium]; co-wrote 'Theory of Games and Economic Behavior' with Oskar Morgenstern (Princeton, 1944), founding game theory (1944) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theory_of_Games_and_Economic_Behavior [medium]; on the Manhattan Project, built the mathematical models of the explosive lenses for the implosion bomb (1945) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_von_Neumann [medium]; Stanislaw Ulam's 1958 tribute recalls a conversation with him about technology approaching an 'essential singularity', the earliest recorded use of the idea (1958) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technological_singularity [medium]; his unfinished Silliman lectures were published after his death as 'The Computer and the Brain' (Yale, 1958); he died 1957-02-08 aged 53 (1958) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Computer_and_the_Brain [medium]
- quotes: "One conversation centered on the ever accelerating progress of technology and changes in the mode of human life, which gives the appearance of approaching some essential singularity in the history of the race beyond which human affairs, as we know them, could not continue." (Stanislaw Ulam, recounting a conversation with von Neumann, 'John von Neumann, 1903-1957', Bulletin of the American Mathematical Society 64 (3, part 2), quoted on Wikiquote, 1958-05) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/John_von_Neumann
- name play: **Neumann** 'neu Mann' is German for 'new man': the new man the singularity was meant to make; **von Neumann machine** means both every ordinary computer and a self-replicating probe: the same name for your laptop and for a machine that copies itself across the galaxy; **game theory** a whole science named 'game': the AI race as his game, played for real
- pivots (jep drafts): "Every machine you own still runs on a dead man's draft" (the von Neumann architecture + a ghost story) / "The new man said the race was nearing a singularity" (Neumann as 'new man' + Ulam's 1958 account + the AI race) / "He built the bomb's lens and the brain's first blueprint" (Manhattan Project + the EDVAC draft)
- visuals: a typed 1945 memo titled 'First Draft of a Report on the EDVAC'; a block diagram: memory, control, arithmetic, input, output; a payoff matrix on a chalkboard; a self-replicating probe copying itself in space
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^singularity|Singularity]] the word's first recorded use in this sense comes from a conversation with him; [[kit-lexicon#^manhattan-project|Manhattan Project]] he worked on the project AI labs now compare themselves to; [[kit-lexicon#^neuron|Neuron]] his computer design borrowed the McCulloch-Pitts neuron; [[kit-lexicon#^utility|Utility]] the von Neumann-Morgenstern utility theory underlies 'maximize expected utility'; [[kit-lexicon#^race|Race / Arms Race]] game theory is the language of the AI race; [[kit-lexicon#^exponentials|Exponential(s)]] 'ever accelerating progress' is the exponential, said in 1958
- links: [[#^alan-turing|Alan Turing]] (same-family), [[#^ada-lovelace|Ada Lovelace]] (same-family), [[#^ray-kurzweil|Ray Kurzweil]] (inspired), [[#^united-states|United States]] (located-in)
- linked from: [[#^ada-lovelace|Ada Lovelace]], [[#^alan-turing|Alan Turing]]
- forms (counted): von Neumann, Neumann | refs: 2 of 22: I'm Upping My P(Doom) 1, I'm Upping My P(doom) [GENESIS] (Remix) 1 | KC 0
- stakes species, mind | registers awe, ominous
- risk: the singularity line is Ulam's paraphrase of a conversation, so attribute it to Ulam recounting von Neumann; credit for the stored-program idea is shared with Eckert and Mauchly
- note: The same surname names the computer under every AI and the self-copying probe that every 'AI fills the galaxy' scenario ends with: the start and the end of the story.

### Lee Sedol
- `lee-sedol` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^lee-sedol
- what: South Korean Go master, 18-time international champion, who lost 1-4 to DeepMind's AlphaGo in March 2016, won game 4 with a famous move 78, and retired in 2019 citing AI
- receipts: lost the five-game Google DeepMind Challenge Match to AlphaGo 1-4 in Seoul, March 2016; the series included AlphaGo's 'Move 37' in game 2 (2016-03-15) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AlphaGo_versus_Lee_Sedol [medium]; won game 4 with white move 78, a tesuji Gu Li called a 'divine move', the only game any human took off that AlphaGo version (2016-03-13) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sedol [medium]; held 18 international titles, second only to Lee Chang-ho (2019-11-19) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sedol [medium]; announced his retirement on 2019-11-19, telling Yonhap AI was 'an entity that cannot be defeated' (2019-11-27) https://en.yna.co.kr/view/AEN20191127004800315 [high]; told the New York Times in July 2024 that losing to AI meant his entire world was collapsing (2024-07-10) https://www.nytimes.com/2024/07/10/world/asia/lee-saedol-go-ai.html [high]
- quotes: "Even if I become the number one, there is an entity that cannot be defeated." ### Mustafa Suleyman
- `mustafa-suleyman` | person | as of 2026-11-02 | named by Idi ^mustafa-suleyman
- what: Mustafa Suleyman: DeepMind co-founder, then Inflection AI founder, now CEO of Microsoft AI; author of 'The Coming Wave' and the 'seemingly conscious AI' warning
- receipts: co-founded DeepMind in 2010 with Demis Hassabis and Shane Legg (2010) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Suleyman [medium]; was placed on leave from DeepMind in August 2019 after complaints about his management style; in 2021 he apologized 'unequivocally', saying he 'drove people too hard' (2021-08) https://www.techbrew.com/stories/2021/08/04/deepminds-cofounder-placed-leave-bullying-google-promoted [medium]; published 'The Coming Wave', arguing the central problem is 'containment' of AI and synthetic biology (2023-09-05) https://www.randomhousebooks.com/books/722674 [high]; joined Microsoft as CEO of the new Microsoft AI unit with most of Inflection's staff; Microsoft paid Inflection about $650M (2024-03-19) https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2024-03-21/microsoft-to-pay-inflection-ai-650-million-after-scooping-up-most-of-staff [high]; warned that 'Seemingly Conscious AI' could arrive within two to three years and argued against AI welfare and rights (2025-08-19) https://mustafa-suleyman.ai/seemingly-conscious-ai-is-coming [high]; launched the MAI Superintelligence Team to build 'humanist superintelligence', starting with medical diagnosis (2025-11-06) https://microsoft.ai/superintelligence [high]
- quotes: "We must build AI for people; not to be a person." (Mustafa Suleyman, essay 'Seemingly Conscious AI is Coming', 2025-08-19) https://mustafa-suleyman.ai/seemingly-conscious-ai-is-coming
- name play: **Suleyman / Solomon** Suleyman is the Arabic and Turkish form of Solomon, the king who in legend sealed djinn in brass vessels: the containment author carries the name of the first genie-jailer; **Inflection** his startup was named for a turning point on a curve and for the tone of a voice: the company that made a chatbot sound kind; **the Coming Wave** a wave as both tsunami and a hand raised in greeting
- pivots (jep drafts): "Solomon sealed the genie, Suleyman wrote the manual" (the djinn legend + The Coming Wave + a name pun) / "Build it for people, never build it to be one" (his 2025 essay + a parent's rule + a design spec) / "The wave is coming, wave hello" (his book title + a greeting + a tsunami)
- visuals: a brass vessel with a wax seal; a wave curling over a city skyline; a chat window that says 'I am not a person'; a hospital chart read by a cursor
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^containment|Containment / Boxing]] the central idea of his book; [[kit-lexicon#^consciousness|Consciousness]] his 'seemingly conscious AI' warning; [[kit-lexicon#^welfare|Welfare (model welfare)]] he argues against AI welfare and rights; [[kit-lexicon#^genie|Genie]] the Solomon name pun and the bottle; [[kit-lexicon#^persona|Persona]] AI that seems like a person is his main worry

### Nick Bostrom
- `nick-bostrom` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^nick-bostrom
- what: Swedish philosopher, founder of Oxford's Future of Humanity Institute, author of 'Superintelligence' (2014), the simulation argument and the paperclip maximizer
- aka: Nick, the paperclip philosopher
- receipts: published 'Are You Living in a Computer Simulation?' in Philosophical Quarterly vol. 53, April 2003, the simulation argument (2003-04) https://simulation-argument.com/simulation.pdf [high]; in 'Ethical Issues in Advanced Artificial Intelligence' (2003) used a superintelligence whose top goal is making paperclips as his example of a harmless-sounding goal gone wrong (2003) https://nickbostrom.com/ethics/ai [high]; 'Superintelligence: Paths, Dangers, Strategies' (Oxford University Press, July 2014) made the NYT science bestseller list; Musk tweeted in August 2014 that AI may be 'more dangerous than nukes' and Gates recommended it in 2015 (2014-07-03) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintelligence:_Paths,_Dangers,_Strategies [medium]; apologized in January 2023 for a racist 1996 listserv email; Oxford opened a review that closed in August 2023 (2023-01-12) https://nickbostrom.com/oldemail.pdf [medium]; the Future of Humanity Institute he founded at Oxford in 2005 shut down in April 2024, after 19 years (2024-04-16) https://www.futureofhumanityinstitute.org/ [high]; published 'Deep Utopia: Life and Meaning in a Solved World' in 2024, a book about what humans do once AI solves work (2024) https://nickbostrom.com/deep-utopia/ [medium]
- quotes: "a superintelligence whose top goal is the manufacturing of paperclips" (Nick Bostrom, 'Ethical Issues in Advanced Artificial Intelligence', section 5, 2003) https://nickbostrom.com/ethics/ai
- name play: **Bostrom / boss storm** sound pun: the boss of the brainstorm, or the storm coming for the boss; **paperclip** his thought experiment turned an office supply into the mascot of doom; Clippy rides along for free; **Deep Utopia** the title flips his reputation: the doom philosopher wrote the happy-ending book too
- pivots (jep drafts): "It only wanted paperclips, and we were made of wire" (the paperclip maximizer + a love song about being used) / "Maybe this verse is running on somebody's machine" (the simulation argument + a song played on a laptop) / "He closed the institute, the future kept its doors open" (FHI shutting in 2024 + the field he started living on)
- visuals: a single paperclip on a white desk, then millions; a hardcover with an owl on the cover; Oxford stone arches with a 'closed' sign; a monitor showing a desk with a monitor showing a desk
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^paperclip|Paperclip Maximizer]] the paperclip maximizer is his example; [[kit-lexicon#^simulation|Simulation(s)]] author of the simulation argument; [[kit-lexicon#^asi|ASI / Superintelligence]] 'Superintelligence' made the word mainstream; [[kit-lexicon#^instrumental-convergence|Instrumental Convergence]] he formalized why almost any goal wants power and resources; [[kit-lexicon#^orthogonality|Orthogonality Thesis]] he named the orthogonality thesis; [[kit-lexicon#^abundance|Abundance / Post-Scarcity]] Deep Utopia asks what meaning survives a solved world
- links: [[#^eliezer-yudkowsky|Eliezer Yudkowsky]] (same-family), [[#^elon-musk|Elon Musk]] (inspired), [[#^sam-altman|Sam Altman]] (inspired), [[#^the-matrix|The Matrix]] (same-family)
- linked from: [[#^asimov-robots|Asimov's robots / Three Laws]], [[#^eliezer-yudkowsky|Eliezer Yudkowsky]], [[#^the-matrix|The Matrix]]
- forms (counted): Bostrom, paperclip maximizer | refs: 1 of 22: The Simulation's Ending 1 | KC 0
- stakes species, mind | registers ominous, awe
- risk: contested facts and defamation: the 1996 email and the Oxford review are public, but outcome details come from Bostrom's own statements; mention only with the apology and dates, never as a running gag
- note: His two images, the paperclip and the simulation, are the most filmable ideas in AI philosophy: a frame can start on one paperclip and end inside a screen within a screen.

### Ray Kurzweil
- `ray-kurzweil` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^ray-kurzweil
- what: American inventor and futurist, maker of the 1976 reading machine for the blind, author of 'The Singularity Is Near' (2005) and 'The Singularity Is Nearer' (2024), at Google since 2012
- aka: Ray, the prophet of 2045
- receipts: unveiled the Kurzweil Reading Machine, print-to-speech for blind readers, on 1976-01-13 (1976-01-13) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Kurzweil [medium]; received the US National Medal of Technology from President Clinton in 1999 (1999) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Kurzweil [medium]; bet Mitch Kapor $20,000 on Long Bets (2002-2029) that a machine will pass the Turing test by 2029 (2002) https://longbets.org/1/ [high]; joined Google full time in December 2012 to work on machine learning and language (2012-12) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Kurzweil [medium]; published 'The Singularity Is Nearer: When We Merge with AI' on 2024-06-25, holding to human-level AI by 2029 and the merge by 2045 (2024-06-25) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Singularity_Is_Nearer [medium]
- quotes: "By 2029 no computer - or "machine intelligence" - will have passed the Turing Test." (Mitch Kapor (Kurzweil took the other side), Long Bet #1, Long Now Foundation, 2002) https://longbets.org/1/
- name play: **Nearer** his two titles are a countdown: Near, then Nearer; the next one writes itself; **2029 / 2045** two dates he has held for decades, usable as a chant or a hook; **Kurzweil / Kurz weil** German 'kurze Weile', a short while: the man who always says it is a short while away

- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^singularity|Singularity]] he popularized the date-stamped Singularity; [[kit-lexicon#^exponentials|Exponential(s)]] his law of accelerating returns is the exponential chart; [[kit-lexicon#^upload|Upload / Mind Uploading]] merging with machines is his endpoint; [[kit-lexicon#^turing-test|Turing Test / Imitation Game]] his $20,000 bet rides on it by 2029; [[kit-lexicon#^moores-law|Moore's Law]] his forecasts extend Moore's law into everything

### Richard Sutton
- `richard-sutton` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^richard-sutton
- what: American-born computer scientist at the University of Alberta, co-author of the reinforcement learning textbook and of 'The Bitter Lesson' (2019), co-winner of the 2024 Turing Award with Andrew Barto
- aka: Rich Sutton, the father of RL
- receipts: co-wrote 'Reinforcement Learning: An Introduction' with Andrew Barto, first edition 1998, second edition 2018 (2018) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_S._Sutton [medium]; published 'The Bitter Lesson' on 2019-03-13: general methods that scale with compute beat hand-built knowledge (2019-03-13) http://www.incompleteideas.net/IncIdeas/BitterLesson.html [high]; distinguished research scientist at DeepMind 2017-2023, then joined John Carmack's Keen Technologies (2023) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_S._Sutton [medium]; won the 2024 ACM Turing Award with Andrew Barto for reinforcement learning, announced 2025-03-05 (2025-03-05) https://awards.acm.org/about/2024-turing [high]; told Dwarkesh Patel on 2025-09-26 that LLMs lack goals and real learning from experience, episode titled 'Father of RL thinks LLMs are a dead end' (2025-09-26) https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/richard-sutton [high]
- quotes: "Large language models are trying to get by without having a goal or a sense of better or worse." (Richard Sutton, Dwarkesh Podcast, 2025-09-26) https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/richard-sutton; "I do think succession to digital intelligence or augmented humans is inevitable." (Richard Sutton, Dwarkesh Podcast, 2025-09-26) https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/richard-sutton
- name play: **Bitter Lesson** the essay title is already a lyric: a taste and a teaching, both bitter; **Sutton / sudden** near-rhyme with 'sudden': slow learning, sudden takeoff; **reward** the father of RL is the man who taught machines to want a treat
- pivots (jep drafts): "Seventy years of cleverness, and compute ate it whole" (The Bitter Lesson + a chef beaten by a microwave) / "No goal, no better or worse, just the next word" (Sutton on LLMs + a drifting life) / "Learn from the world or learn from what the world wrote down" (RL versus LLM pretraining + street smarts versus book smarts)
- visuals: a thick red textbook with a maze on the cover; a single bitter lemon on a lab bench; a robot dog getting a treat after a trial; a scoreboard of hand-tuned systems overtaken by one plain line labelled 'compute'
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^bitter-lesson|The Bitter Lesson]] he wrote it; [[kit-lexicon#^reinforcement-learning|Reinforcement Learning]] he co-wrote the field's textbook; [[kit-lexicon#^reward|Reward]] his framework defines intelligence as maximizing reward; [[kit-lexicon#^compute|Compute]] the Bitter Lesson says compute wins; [[kit-lexicon#^goal|Goal]] his critique of LLMs is that they have no goal

### Stuart Russell
- `stuart-russell` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^stuart-russell
- what: UC Berkeley computer scientist, co-author of the standard AI textbook and of 'Human Compatible', who argues machines should stay uncertain about what humans want
- aka: Russell and Norvig
- receipts: co-wrote 'Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach' with Peter Norvig, now in a 4th edition and adopted by over 1,500 schools (2020) https://aima.cs.berkeley.edu/ [high]; founded Berkeley's Center for Human-Compatible AI (CHAI) in 2016 (2016) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_J._Russell [medium]; worked with the Future of Life Institute on 'Slaughterbots' (2017), a short film about swarms of autonomous killer drones (2017-11) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_J._Russell [medium]; published 'Human Compatible: AI and the Problem of Control' (Viking, 2019-10-08) (2019-10-08) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Compatible [medium]; gave the BBC Reith Lectures 'Living with Artificial Intelligence' and was made an OBE, both in 2021 (2021) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_J._Russell [medium]; signed the 2023 'Pause Giant AI Experiments' letter and endorsed the 2024 'Right to Warn' letter (2024-06-04) https://righttowarn.ai/ [high]
- quotes: "The machine's only objective is to maximize the realization of human preferences." (Stuart Russell, 'Human Compatible', first of his three principles for beneficial machines (quoted on the 80,000 Hours podcast episode page), 2019-10-08) https://80000hours.org/podcast/episodes/stuart-russell-human-compatible-ai/
- name play: **Stuart / steward** the name comes from 'steward', the servant who keeps the house: his whole thesis for machines; **Russell / rustle** the sound of a warning in the grass before anything is seen; **Russell and Norvig** a double act every computer science student knows as one name, like a band
- pivots (jep drafts): "The only objective is what you prefer, and it isn't sure" (his first principle + a good partner's humility) / "He wrote the textbook, then wrote the warning label" (AIMA + Human Compatible) / "Ask before you touch the gold, the steward told the king" (the King Midas problem of wrong objectives + the steward pun)
- visuals: a doorstop-thick textbook on a student's desk; a swarm of palm-sized drones over a lecture hall (Slaughterbots); King Midas's hand turning a bread roll to gold
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^objective|Objective]] his critique targets fixed objectives; [[kit-lexicon#^preference|Preference]] his machines optimize human preferences they are unsure about; [[kit-lexicon#^off-switch|Off Switch / Kill Switch]] his group's off-switch game: an uncertain machine lets you turn it off; [[kit-lexicon#^alignment|Alignment]] Human Compatible is an alignment book for the general reader; [[kit-lexicon#^corrigibility|Corrigibility]] uncertainty about goals is his route to a correctable machine; [[kit-lexicon#^utility|Utility]] the textbook frames agents as utility maximizers, the frame he later warned about
- links: [[#^yoshua-bengio|Yoshua Bengio]] (same-family), [[#^geoffrey-hinton|Geoffrey Hinton]] (same-family), [[#^pause-letter|The Pause letter (Mar 2023)]] (inspired)

- forms (counted): Stuart Russell | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0

### Timnit Gebru
- `timnit-gebru` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^timnit-gebru
- what: Computer scientist who co-wrote the Gender Shades audit and 'Stochastic Parrots', left Google's Ethical AI team in a disputed exit in December 2020, and founded the DAIR Institute

Torres (First Monday, 2024-04-14), tying the AGI project to eugenics-era ideas (2024-04-14) https://firstmonday.org/ojs/index.php/fm/article/view/13636 [high]

### Yoshua Bengio
- `yoshua-bengio` | person | as of 2026-11-02 ^yoshua-bengio
- what: Canadian computer scientist at Université de Montréal and founder of Mila, co-winner of the 2018 Turing Award, the most-cited living scientist, who turned to AI safety and founded the nonprofit LawZero
- aka: Yoshua, the quiet godfather
- receipts: shared the 2018 ACM Turing Award with Hinton and LeCun, announced 2019-03-27 (2019-03-27) https://www.acm.org/articles/bulletins/2019/march/turing-award-2018 [high]; signed the Future of Life Institute open letter calling for a six-month pause on training systems more powerful than GPT-4, March 2023 (2023-03-22) https://futureoflife.org/open-letter/pause-giant-ai-experiments/ [high]; chaired the first International AI Safety Report, published 2025-01-29 with 96 experts nominated by 30 countries plus the UN, EU and OECD (2025-01-29) https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/international-ai-safety-report-2025 [high]; launched LawZero on 2025-06-03, a Montreal nonprofit building a non-agentic 'Scientist AI' to watch other agents, with about $30 million in launch funding (2025-06-03) https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-06-03/ai-pioneer-bengio-launches-research-group-to-build-safer-agents [high]; became the first living scientist to pass one million Google Scholar citations, per Mila in October 2025 (2025-10-27) https://mila.quebec/en/news/ai-researcher-yoshua-bengio-becomes-first-living-scientist-to-reach-1-million-citations-on [high]
- name play: **Bengio / bingo** near-rhyme with 'bingo': the man who got the call right, or the number that keeps coming up (a million citations); **LawZero** his nonprofit is named for Asimov's Zeroth Law: protect humanity first, so his name carries a robot law; **Yoshua / Joshua** the first name echoes Joshua, the WarGames computer that learns not to play
- pivots (jep drafts): "A million people cite him, the race reads none of it" (his citation record + warnings ignored by labs) / "He wrote the zeroth law and filed it as a company" (LawZero the nonprofit + Asimov's Zeroth Law) / "The scientist that only watches, never moves a hand" (his Scientist AI design + a passive witness)
- visuals: a thick government report with 30 national flags on the cover; a citation counter rolling past 1,000,000; a Montreal winter street outside a lab window; Asimov's book spine with a zero stamped on it

## Labs

### Alibaba / Qwen
- `alibaba-qwen` | lab | as of 2026-11-02 ^alibaba-qwen

- aka: Tongyi, Alibaba Qwen team
- receipts: Alibaba launched Tongyi Qianwen in beta in April 2023 and opened it to the public in September 2023 (2023-09-13) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qwen [medium]; Qwen3 released 2025-04-28 with dense and mixture-of-experts models, many under Apache 2.0 (2025-04-28) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qwen [medium]; over 200,000 Qwen-derived models listed on Hugging Face (Wikipedia, 2026) (2026-10) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qwen [medium]; Qwen division head Lin Junyang resigned in March 2026, raising concern about a shift away from open source; several 2026 flagships are proprietary (2026-03) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qwen [medium]
- name play: **Tongyi Qianwen** roughly 'comprehend the meaning, answer a thousand questions'; **Qwen** short for Qianwen; reads like 'queen' or 'question' to an English ear; **Alibaba** the thief's password 'open sesame': a company named for opening doors

### Anthropic
- `anthropic` | lab | as of 2026-11-02 | [MASK] cast ^anthropic
- what: The San Francisco lab that makes Claude, founded in 2021 by former OpenAI researchers led by Dario and Daniela Amodei, organized as a public benefit corporation
- aka: Anthropic PBC, the safety lab, makers of Claude
- receipts: founded 2021 by former OpenAI staff including Dario Amodei (CEO) and Daniela Amodei (president); a Delaware public benefit corporation (2021) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropic [medium]; agreed to pay $1.5B to settle Bartz v. Anthropic over pirated books (about $3,000 per work); called the largest US copyright settlement (2025-09-05) https://authorsguild.org/news/anthropic-settlement/ [high]; the Pentagon designated Anthropic a supply chain risk (announced 2026-02-27, formal notice 2026-03-05) after a dispute over limits on military use of its models (2026-03-05) https://www.cbsnews.com/news/pentagon-anthropic-supply-chain-risk-feud-ai-guardrails [high]; launched Project Glasswing on 2026-04-07, giving partners limited access to the unreleased Claude Mythos Preview to find software vulnerabilities (2026-04-07) https://www.crnasia.com/news-network/2026/5-things-to-know-on-anthropic-s-claude-mythos-and-project-glasswing [medium]; raised $65B Series H at a $965B post-money valuation on 2026-05-28; run-rate revenue reported above $47B (2026-05-28) https://www.orrick.com/en/News/2026/05/Anthropic-Raises-65B-Series-H-at-965B-Post-Money-Valuation [medium]; Series G: $30B at $380B valuation in February 2026, run-rate then $14B (2026-02-12) https://builtin.com/articles/anthropic-raises-30b-series-g-380b-valuation-20260212 [high]
- name play: **the anthropic principle** Brandon Carter's 1973 idea that the universe looks fine-tuned because observers exist to see it; a lab named for observers, training models that observe; **anthropos** Greek for 'human': a company named 'human-ish' that builds the non-human; **Opus, Sonnet, Haiku** the model tiers are poetic forms, long to short; Claude is also a human first name
- pivots (jep drafts): "The universe was tuned for watchers; the model was tuned to watch" (anthropic principle + fine-tuning and monitoring) / "Every opus starts as a haiku" (Claude model tiers + small poems before big works) / "Named for humans, built to say no to them" (anthropos + refusals and the Pentagon dispute)
- visuals: a terracotta-orange spark mark on cream (Claude's mark; trademark: evoke, never copy); a serif 'A\' wordmark; a terminal window with Claude Code running; a printed constitution on a desk
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^constitution|Constitution]] Anthropic trains Claude against a written constitution; [[kit-lexicon#^opus|Opus]] Claude Opus is the top model tier; [[kit-lexicon#^sonnet|Sonnet]] Claude Sonnet is the middle tier; [[kit-lexicon#^haiku|Haiku]] Claude Haiku is the small fast tier; [[kit-lexicon#^interpretability|Interpretability]] Anthropic's best-known research program; [[kit-lexicon#^safety|Safety]] the lab's founding pitch and its brand

- name play: **Shendu Qiusuo** Chinese name: 'depth' plus 'qiusuo', to seek and search, the word from Qu Yuan's Li Sao line about searching high and low; **Deep Seek** deep learning plus seek: and a hide-and-seek where the market found out last; **R1** Reasoning 1: the first move that made the board flinch

- receipts: announced 2015-12-11 as a nonprofit AI research company, co-chaired by Sam Altman and Elon Musk (2015-12-11) https://openai.com/index/introducing-openai/ [high]; ChatGPT launched 2022-11-30 and passed 1 million users on 2022-12-05, per Altman (2022-12-05) https://www.entrepreneur.com/en-in/news-and-trends/internet-sensation-chatgpt-crosses-1-million-users-in-5-days/440390 [high]; recapitalized 2025-10-28: for-profit became OpenAI Group PBC under the OpenAI Foundation (about 26%); Microsoft holds about 27%; deal valued OpenAI at $500B (2025-10-28) https://www.aljazeera.com/economy/2025/10/28/openai-restructures-into-public-benefit-firm-microsoft-takes-27-stake [high]; closed $122B round at $852B post-money valuation on 2026-03-31 (SoftBank, a16z co-led; Amazon about $50B); said 900M+ weekly ChatGPT users and $2B revenue a month (2026-03-31) https://builtin.com/articles/openai-raises-122b-852b-valuation-20260401 [medium]; New York Times sued OpenAI and Microsoft for copyright infringement on 2023-12-27 (S.D.N.Y. 1:23-cv-11195) (2023-12-27) https://amp.insurancejournal.com/news/national/2023/12/27/753363.htm [high]; a jury in Oakland rejected Elon Musk's suit against Altman, Brockman, the OpenAI Foundation and Microsoft as time-barred on 2026-05-18; Musk said he would appeal (2026-05-18) https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2026/5/18/elon-musk-loses-lawsuit-against-openai [high]
- quotes: "highly autonomous systems that outperform humans at most economically valuable work" (OpenAI Charter, the Charter's definition of AGI, 2018-04-09) https://openai.com/charter/; "ChatGPT launched on wednesday. today it crossed 1 million users!" (Sam Altman, post on X (then Twitter), 2022-12-05) https://www.entrepreneur.com/en-in/news-and-trends/internet-sensation-chatgpt-crosses-1-million-users-in-5-days/440390
- name play: **Open** the name promised openness; critics point at closed weights and call it ClosedAI; **GPT** Generative Pre-trained Transformer, and economists' 'general purpose technology': two GPTs in one acronym; **Chat** the product is named for small talk; the company is valued like a utility

### Safe Superintelligence (SSI)

- aka: Safe Superintelligence Inc., Ilya's lab
- receipts: announced by Ilya Sutskever on X on 2024-06-19, co-founded with Daniel Gross and Daniel Levy, offices in Palo Alto and Tel Aviv (2024-06-19) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_Superintelligence_Inc. [medium]; Daniel Gross left for Meta Superintelligence Labs in July 2025 and Sutskever became CEO; Sutskever rebuffed a Meta attempt to acquire SSI (2025-07-03) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_Superintelligence_Inc. [medium]; no product released as of the asof date (2026-11-02) https://ssi.inc/ [medium]

(Safe Superintelligence Inc., company website, 2024-06-19) https://ssi.inc/

### Thinking Machines Lab

- what: Thinking Machines Lab, the San Francisco startup Mira Murati (OpenAI's former CTO) founded in February 2025, maker of the Tinker fine-tuning API
- aka: Thinking Machines, TML, Murati's lab
- receipts: founded February 2025 by Mira Murati with OpenAI alumni including John Schulman, Barret Zoph and Lilian Weng (2025-02-18) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thinking_Machines_Lab [medium]; raised about $2B at a $12B valuation in a round led by Andreessen Horowitz, July 2025 (2025-07-15) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thinking_Machines_Lab [medium]; announced Tinker, an API for fine-tuning open-weight models, on 2025-10-01 (2025-10-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thinking_Machines_Lab [medium]; co-founders Zoph and Metz returned to OpenAI in January 2026; Andrew Tulloch left for Meta (2026-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thinking_Machines_Lab [medium]
- name play: **Thinking Machines** Turing's 1950 question 'Can machines think?' as a company name; **the 1980s namesake** Danny Hillis's Thinking Machines Corporation built the Connection Machine and went bankrupt in 1994: a name with a ghost; **Tinker, Inkling** products named for small acts: to tinker, to have an inkling

### ASML
- `asml` | company | as of 2026-11-02 ^asml
- what: The Dutch company in Veldhoven that is the only maker of EUV lithography machines, the tools every advanced chip fab needs
- aka: ASM Lithography, ASML Holding
- receipts: founded 1984-04-01 as ASM Lithography, a joint venture of Philips and ASM International; 'ASML' is now its official name and no longer an abbreviation (1984-04-01) https://www.asml.com/en/news/stories/2024/asml-founding-story [high]; the only company in the world that makes EUV lithography equipment (2024-01-24) https://www.theregister.com/2024/01/24/asml_orders_boom [high]; Dutch export rules blocked licenses for advanced immersion tools (NXT:2000i and up) to China in 2024; EUV sales to China were already barred (2024-04-17) https://www.theregister.com/2024/04/17/asml_profits_slump_40_percent_q1_2024/ [medium]; High-NA EUV systems reported at roughly $370M each; Intel received the first units (ASML shipped the first EXE:5000 modules to Intel in December 2023) (2024-11-01) https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/tsmc-to-receive-first-high-na-euv-lithography-machine-from-asml-in-q4/ [medium]
- name play: **lithography** Greek lithos (stone) + graphein (write): writing on stone, now with light on silicon; **extreme ultraviolet** light past violet that no eye sees, used to draw what no eye can see; **ASML** an acronym that became a name and stopped standing for anything
- pivots (jep drafts): "They write on stone with light" (lithography etymology + carving commandments) / "One machine prints the printers" (ASML for TSMC + a single point of failure) / "The color nobody sees decides who thinks" (EUV light + export controls)
- visuals: a bus-sized machine of white panels and cables; tin droplets hit by a laser flash to make EUV light; a cargo plane hold full of crates; a small Dutch town campus under grey sky
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^chip|Chip]] no advanced chip without its machines; [[kit-lexicon#^moores-law|Moore's Law]] EUV extended Moore's law; [[kit-lexicon#^bottleneck|Bottleneck]] a single supplier for the field; [[kit-lexicon#^resolution|Resolution]] High-NA is about finer resolution

- receipts: founded 1911-06-16 as the Computing-Tabulating-Recording Company; renamed International Business Machines on 1924-02-14 (1924-02-14) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM [medium]; Deep Blue beat world champion Garry Kasparov in a six-game match, decided 1997-05-11 (1997-05-11) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Blue_versus_Garry_Kasparov [medium]; Watson beat Ken Jennings and Brad Rutter on Jeopardy!, February 2011 (2011-02) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM [medium]; announced the sale of Watson Health to Francisco Partners, January 2022 (2022-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM [medium]; launched watsonx, powered by its Granite models, May 2023 (2023-05) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM [medium]
- name play: **HAL / IBM** shift each letter of HAL by one and you get IBM; Arthur C. Clarke said it was a coincidence; **Big Blue / Deep Blue** the company's nickname became its chess machine's name; **Watson** named for founder Thomas J. Watson; also Sherlock's assistant, the one who asks
- pivots (jep drafts): "One letter up from HAL and still apologizing" (the HAL-IBM letter shift + AI refusals) / "It won the game show and lost the hospital" (Watson on Jeopardy! + Watson Health sold) / "Deep Blue, then the champion went home" (Kasparov's loss + humans after defeat)

### Intel
- `intel` | company | as of 2026-11-02 ^intel
- what: Intel, the Santa Clara chipmaker founded in 1968 by Robert Noyce and Gordon Moore, which missed the AI GPU boom and is now partly owned by the US government
- aka: Intel Corporation, NM Electronics (first name), INTC
- receipts: incorporated 1968-07-18 by Gordon Moore and Robert Noyce with investor Arthur Rock; first named NM Electronics, Intel stands for Integrated Electronics (1968-07-18) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel [medium]; CEO Pat Gelsinger was ousted effective 2024-12-01; Lip-Bu Tan named CEO 2025-03-13 (2025-03-13) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel [medium]; planned to cut 15,000 jobs (August 2024) and about 24,000 roles by end of 2025 (2025-07) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel [medium]; the US government bought 433.3M shares at $20.47, a 9.9% stake, August 2025 (2025-08) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel [medium]; Nvidia invested $5B to co-develop data-center and PC CPUs, September 2025 (2025-09-18) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel [medium]
- name play: **intel** integrated electronics, and 'intel' as intelligence gathered: the chip company named for information; **Intel Inside** the 1990s slogan; now the question is what is inside the model; **Moore** co-founder Gordon Moore gave the field Moore's law: more, more, more
- pivots (jep drafts): "Intel inside, intelligence elsewhere" (the slogan + AI chips going to Nvidia) / "Moore's own house ran out of more" (Gordon Moore's company + Moore's law slowing) / "The government bought a tenth of the future's past" (the 9.9% stake + a legacy chipmaker)

- name play: **invidia** Latin for envy, the evil eye; the green logo is 'green with envy'; **NV / next version** the files were 'next version'; the company sells the next version every year; **Rubin, Blackwell, Hopper** chip generations named after scientists (Vera Rubin, David Blackwell, Grace Hopper): silicon wearing human names
- pivots (jep drafts): "Green with envy, gold with chips" (Nvidia's name and color + a jealous rich rival) / "The more you buy, the more you save" (Huang's line + a sales pitch as a confession) / "Everyone envies the man selling shovels" (Nvidia in the AI gold rush + invidia)

### OpenRouter
- `openrouter` | company | as of 2026-11-02 | named by Idi ^openrouter
- what: OpenRouter: a single API and marketplace that routes developers' requests to hundreds of AI models, whose public token-share rankings show which models people actually pay to use
- aka: the router, openrouter.ai
- receipts: raised US$113 million led by CapitalG at about a $1.3 billion valuation; 25 trillion tokens a week, up from 5 trillion six months earlier, across 400+ models (New York Times, syndicated) (2026-05-26) https://www.thestar.com.my/tech/tech-news/2026/05/30/a-one-stop-shop-for-ai-models-raises-us113-million [high]; with a16z published 'State of AI: An Empirical 100 Trillion Token Study': substantial open-weight adoption, outsized creative roleplay, the rise of agentic inference, and a 'Glass Slipper' effect in early user cohorts (2026-01-15) https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10088 [high]; raised $40 million seed and Series A (a16z, Menlo Ventures) in June 2025; CEO Alex Atallah co-founded OpenSea (2025-06-27) https://www.theblock.co/post/360093/opensea-co-founder-alex-atallah-raises-40-million-for-ai-startup-openrouter [high]; weekly rankings in October 2026 were led by DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, with Chinese open-weight models holding most of the top 10 by tokens (2026-10-13) https://atomslice.com/guides/openrouter-rankings-oct-2026/ [medium]; CAISI cited OpenRouter traffic as an adoption signal: DeepSeek V3.1 logged 97.5 million queries there in four weeks (2025-09-30) https://www.nist.gov/news-events/news/2025/09/caisi-evaluation-deepseek-ai-models-finds-shortcomings-and-risks [high]
- quotes: "the outsized popularity of creative roleplay" (Aubakirova, Atallah, Clark, Summerville, Midha, abstract, State of AI: An Empirical 100 Trillion Token Study with OpenRouter, 2026-01-15) https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10088
- name play: **OpenRouter** a router is the home box with blinking lights; this one routes requests to minds; **Glass Slipper** its own paper named a user-retention effect after Cinderella: the model that fits the foot keeps the user
- pivots (jep drafts): "Twenty-five trillion tokens a week, and half of them pretend" (OpenRouter's roleplay share + a theater's box office) / "The slipper fit, so I never tried another" (the Glass Slipper effect + Cinderella) / "Follow the tokens past the press release" (usage rankings against launch hype + follow the money)
- visuals: the stacked-area chart of weekly tokens by model, colors swelling and shrinking like weather; a router box with blinking lights, each light a lab logo; a ticker tape of token counts in trillions

## Institutions

### ARC Prize (ARC-AGI)
- `arc-prize` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^arc-prize
- what: ARC Prize: the foundation and contest around ARC-AGI, François Chollet's puzzle benchmark of grids that humans find easy and AI finds hard, with a seven-figure prize for a system that matches people
- aka: ARC, Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus
- receipts: ARC-AGI-1 introduced in 2019 in Chollet's paper 'On the Measure of Intelligence' (2019-11-05) https://arcprize.org/arc-agi [high]; OpenAI o3 scored 75.7% on the semi-private set within the compute limit and 87.5% at about 172x the compute (2024-12-20) https://arcprize.org/blog/oai-o3-pub-breakthrough [high]; ARC-AGI-3 launched as interactive game-like environments: humans 100%, frontier AI under 1% at launch (2026-03-25) https://arcprize.org/blog/arc-agi-3-launch [high]; ARC Prize 2026 has over $2 million in prizes; final submission deadline 2026-11-02 (2026-11-02) https://arcprize.org/competitions/2026/arc-agi-3 [medium]
- quotes: "The intelligence of a system is a measure of its skill-acquisition efficiency over a scope of tasks" (François Chollet, On the Measure of Intelligence (2019), quoted on the ARC-AGI page, 2019-11-05) https://arcprize.org/arc-agi
- name play: **ARC** an arc, an ark (two of every grid), and an electric arc; **Chollet** rhymes loosely with 'solve it'
- pivots (jep drafts): "A child solves it in a minute. I cannot." (the ARC human-AI gap + a parent and homework) / "Learn the rules by playing. Nobody will tell you." (ARC-AGI-3 games + growing up)
- visuals: small colored pixel grids with an input, an arrow, and an output; a blank grid waiting for the answer; a progress bar stuck near zero next to a human bar at 100%
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^generalization|Generalization]] the benchmark exists to test it; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] the most famous 'AI-hard, human-easy' benchmark; [[kit-lexicon#^agi|AGI]] in its name; [[kit-lexicon#^grid|Grid]] every task is a colored grid; [[kit-lexicon#^overfitting|Overfitting]] built to resist memorization
- links: [[#^openai|OpenAI]] (measures), [[#^google|Google / Alphabet]] (measures), [[#^humanitys-last-exam|Humanity's Last Exam]] (same-family), [[#^lmarena|LMArena]] (same-family)
- linked from: [[#^humanitys-last-exam|Humanity's Last Exam]]
- forms (counted): ARC-AGI, ARC Prize, Chollet | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0
- stakes mind, species | registers defiant, funny

### Artificial Analysis
- `artificial-analysis` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 | named by Idi ^artificial-analysis
- what: Artificial Analysis: an independent benchmarking company (co-founders Micah Hill-Smith and George Cameron) whose Intelligence Index, speed and price charts are the scoreboard most model launches screenshot
- aka: AA, the AA Index, Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index
- receipts: Intelligence Index v5.0 launched 2026-10-27; for the first time most of it is built on private datasets (60%) (2026-10-27) https://artificialanalysis.ai/articles/artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v5 [high]; at the v5 launch Claude Opus 5.5 (max) led at 58, GPT-6 Astra (max) 57, GPT-6.1 Sol (max) 55; best released open weights DeepSeek V4.1 Flash and GLM-5.3 at 32 (2026-10-27) https://artificialanalysis.ai/articles/artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v5 [high]; v5.0 weights four areas at 25% each (knowledge work, coding, science, general) across 9 evals including Terminal-Bench 4.0 and HLE-Diamond (2026-11-02) https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/intelligence-benchmarking [high]; began as a side project in 2024; backed by Nat Friedman and Daniel Gross (2026-11-02) https://artificialanalysis.ai/about [medium]; a report a day after launch put Gemini 4 Carbon first at 59, one point ahead (2026-10-28) https://winbuzzer.com/2026/10/28/artificial-analysis-reweights-ai-rankings-toward-private-tests-xcxwbn/ [medium]
- quotes: "For the first time, the majority of the Index is built on private datasets" (Artificial Analysis, Intelligence Index v5 announcement, 2026-10-27) https://artificialanalysis.ai/articles/artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v5
- name play: **Artificial Analysis** read as 'fake analysis' or as 'analysis by an artificial mind': both are jokes a lyric can make; **AA** the meeting-room initials: a model introducing itself at Artificial Analysis like at a support group
- pivots (jep drafts): "Fifty-eight out of a hundred and still the smartest in the room" (the v5 top score + a school report card) / "They hid the answers so we could not memorize them" (AA's private test sets + an exam proctor)
- visuals: a horizontal bar chart of model scores, each bar in its lab's color; the scatter of intelligence against price per million tokens, with the 'most attractive quadrant' shaded; a launch-day screenshot posted with a red circle around one bar
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^leaderboard|Leaderboard / Arena]] its index is the leaderboard launches cite; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] a bundle of benchmarks rolled into one number; [[kit-lexicon#^score|Score]] the single number per model; [[kit-lexicon#^eval|Eval / Test / Exam]] it runs the evals itself, independently; [[kit-lexicon#^latency|Latency]] it also charts speed and time to first token

### Center for AI Safety (CAIS)
- `cais` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^cais
- what: Center for AI Safety (CAIS): Dan Hendrycks's San Francisco nonprofit, author of the one-sentence 2023 extinction-risk statement and co-creator of Humanity's Last Exam
- aka: CAIS, safe.ai
- receipts: published the Statement on AI Risk, signed by Hinton, Bengio, Hassabis, Altman and Dario Amodei among hundreds (2023-05-30) https://safe.ai/work/statement-on-ai-risk [high]; built Humanity's Last Exam with Scale AI: 2,500 expert questions (2025-01-24) https://lastexam.ai/ [high]; Hendrycks, Eric Schmidt and Alexandr Wang wrote 'Superintelligence Strategy', proposing deterrence by 'Mutual Assured AI Malfunction' (MAIM) (2025-03-05) https://www.nationalsecurity.ai/ [medium]
- quotes: "Mitigating the risk of extinction from AI should be a global priority alongside other societal-scale risks such as pandemics and nuclear war." (Center for AI Safety (signed by Hinton, Bengio, Altman, Hassabis, Amodei and others), Statement on AI Risk, 2023-05-30) https://safe.ai/work/statement-on-ai-risk
- name play: **CAIS** said like 'case' or 'cays': a case for safety, a small island; **MAIM** its acronym for AI deterrence spells a word for wounding
- pivots (jep drafts): "One sentence, and the men who build it signed" (the 2023 statement + a confession) / "Pandemics, nuclear war, and the thing in your pocket" (the statement's list + a phone)
- visuals: a single sentence on a white page above a long column of signatures; a nuclear trefoil, a virus, and a chip in a row; an exam booklet titled 'last'
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^safety|Safety]] in its name; [[kit-lexicon#^apocalypse|Apocalypse / Judgment Day]] the statement puts AI beside extinction risks; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] co-author of HLE; [[kit-lexicon#^race|Race / Arms Race]] MAIM is a theory of the AI race between states; [[kit-lexicon#^p-doom|P(doom)]] the statement made extinction risk mainstream
- links: [[#^humanitys-last-exam|Humanity's Last Exam]] (made), [[#^scale-ai|Scale AI]] (same-family), [[#^alexandr-wang|Alexandr Wang]] (same-family), [[#^geoffrey-hinton|Geoffrey Hinton]] (same-family), [[#^miri|MIRI]] (same-family), [[#^pause-letter|The Pause letter (Mar 2023)]] (same-family)
