### Epoch AI
- `epoch-ai` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^epoch-ai
- what: Epoch AI: a research nonprofit that tracks the trends behind AI (training compute, data stocks, hardware, cost) and runs the FrontierMath benchmark
- aka: Epoch
- receipts: training compute of notable models grew 4-5x per year from 2010 to May 2024 (frontier: about 5.3x) (2024-05-28) https://epoch.ai/blog/training-compute-of-frontier-ai-models-grows-by-4-5x-per-year [high]; estimated the stock of public human text at about 300 trillion effective tokens, likely exhausted by training runs between 2026 and 2032 (2024-06-04) https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.04325 [high]; OpenAI funded FrontierMath and had access to most problems except a holdout set; Epoch disclosed it only on 2024-12-20, the day o3 claimed about 25% (2025-01-23) https://epoch.ai/blog/openai-and-frontiermath [high]; criticized in January 2025 for waiting to disclose the OpenAI funding; contributors said they had not been told (2025-01-19) https://techcrunch.com/2025/01/19/ai-benchmarking-organization-criticized-for-waiting-to-disclose-funding-from-openai [high]
- name play: **Epoch** an era, and one pass through the training data: the lab is named after both a geological age and a training step; **FrontierMath** math at the edge of the map, where the dragons are
- pivots (jep drafts): "One more epoch and we run out of words" (Epoch's data-exhaustion forecast + a training pass + an era ending) / "Four times the fire every year" (Epoch's compute trend + a growing blaze)
- visuals: the log-scale scatter of training compute (FLOP) by year, dots rising in a straight band; a library shelf emptying as a progress bar fills; a chalkboard of unsolved equations labelled FrontierMath
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^epoch|Epoch]] shares the name with the training term; [[kit-lexicon#^compute|Compute]] its central trend line; [[kit-lexicon#^flops|FLOPs]] its charts are measured in FLOP; [[kit-lexicon#^scaling|Scale / Scaling (laws)]] it is the bookkeeper of scaling; [[kit-lexicon#^synthetic-data|Synthetic Data]] the data-wall forecast pushes labs toward synthetic data; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] FrontierMath
- links: [[#^openai|OpenAI]] (measures), [[#^metr|METR]] (same-family), [[#^artificial-analysis|Artificial Analysis]] (same-family), [[#^ai-2027|AI 2027 (the scenario)]] (inspired)
- linked from: [[#^arxiv|arXiv]], [[#^metr|METR]]

### Humanity's Last Exam
- `humanitys-last-exam` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^humanitys-last-exam
- what: Humanity's Last Exam (HLE): a 2,500-question expert benchmark from the Center for AI Safety and Scale AI, built to be the last closed-ended academic test AI would need, published in Nature in 2026
- aka: HLE-Diamond, HLE-Rolling, the last exam
- receipts: arXiv 2501.14249 (posted 2025-01-24): 2,500 questions across dozens of subjects; frontier models showed low accuracy and calibration (2025-01-24) https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14249 [high]; finalized at 2,500 questions on 2025-04-03; published in Nature (vol. 649) on 2026-01-28 (2026-01-28) https://lastexam.ai/ [high]; HLE-Diamond released 2026-09-22 as a migration path once frontier AI hits the noise ceiling on the original (2026-09-22) https://lastexam.ai/ [high]; Artificial Analysis v5 swapped HLE for HLE-Diamond in its Intelligence Index (2026-10-27) https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/intelligence-benchmarking [high]
- quotes: "designed to be the final closed-ended academic benchmark of its kind with broad subject coverage" (Phan, Gatti, Han, Li et al. (CAIS and Scale AI), Humanity's Last Exam, abstract, 2025-01-24) https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14249
- name play: **Humanity's Last Exam** the last exam humans can set, or the last exam humanity sits: the title is a pun on extinction; **noise ceiling** the 'last' exam got a sequel within two years
- pivots (jep drafts): "This is the last exam, and I am the one sitting it" (the benchmark + a final judgment) / "They wrote the hardest questions, then needed harder ones" (HLE to HLE-Diamond + a teacher outpaced) / "Pencils down, humanity" (the benchmark title + an ending)
- visuals: an exam hall with one robot at a desk and rows of empty chairs; a stack of 2,500 index cards; a bar chart of model scores creeping up a tall empty axis
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] the canonical 'hardest' benchmark of 2025; [[kit-lexicon#^eval|Eval / Test / Exam]] a closed-ended academic eval; [[kit-lexicon#^overfitting|Overfitting]] a private held-out set guards against it; [[kit-lexicon#^score|Score]] scores quoted at every launch; [[kit-lexicon#^plateau|Plateau]] the 'noise ceiling' it now approaches

### ImageNet
- `imagenet` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^imagenet
- what: ImageNet: Fei-Fei Li's dataset of over 14 million hand-labelled images, whose 2012 contest win by AlexNet on GPUs started the deep-learning revolution
- aka: ILSVRC, the ImageNet challenge
- receipts: over 14 million hand-annotated images across 20,000+ categories, first presented as a poster at CVPR 2009 (2009-06-20) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ImageNet [medium]; labelled on Amazon Mechanical Turk by 49,000 workers from 167 countries, July 2008 to April 2010 (2010-04-30) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ImageNet [medium]; AlexNet reached a 15.3% top-5 error in the 2012 challenge, more than 10.8 points ahead of the runner-up, trained on GPUs (2012-09-30) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ImageNet [medium]; Karpathy estimated a human could reach about 5.1% error with effort; Microsoft's 100+ layer network hit 3.57% in 2015 (2015-12-10) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ImageNet [medium]
- name play: **ImageNet** a net that catches images: fifteen million fish in one haul; **Mechanical Turk** the labelling was done on a service named after an 18th-century fake chess robot with a man hidden inside
- pivots (jep drafts): "Forty-nine thousand hands taught the machine to see" (ImageNet's labellers + a blind man's friends) / "A hidden human inside every clever machine" (Mechanical Turk labelling + the original Turk hoax) / "Twenty twelve, the year the error fell" (AlexNet's win + a fall from grace)
- visuals: a mosaic wall of thumbnails, each with a one-word label; a bar chart of contest error rates falling year by year below the human line; two GTX 580 graphics cards on a desk
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^label|Label]] a dataset made of labels; [[kit-lexicon#^ground-truth|Ground Truth]] the labels are the ground truth; [[kit-lexicon#^benchmark|Benchmark]] the benchmark that defined a decade; [[kit-lexicon#^pixel|Pixel(s)]] pixels in, a word out; [[kit-lexicon#^bitter-lesson|The Bitter Lesson]] data plus compute beat hand-built features
- links: [[#^fei-fei-li|Fei-Fei Li]] (founded), [[#^geoffrey-hinton|Geoffrey Hinton]] (same-family), [[#^ilya-sutskever|Ilya Sutskever]] (same-family), [[#^nvidia|Nvidia]] (same-family), [[#^andrej-karpathy|Andrej Karpathy]] (measures)

### LMArena
- `lmarena` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^lmarena

(formed 2025), $250 million raised in total; created by UC Berkeley researchers (2026-11-02) https://arena.ai/about [high]; 'The Leaderboard Illusion' (Cohere and others) found undisclosed private testing: Meta tested 27 variants in one month before Llama 4 (2025-04-29) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20879 [high]; the Llama 4 model that topped the leaderboard was a different version from the one Meta released (2025-04-30) https://simonwillison.net/2025/Apr/30/criticism-of-the-chatbot-arena/ [high]

- name play: **Arena** two models enter, one model leaves: gladiators judged by a crowd's thumbs; **Elo** a chess rating applied to chatbots; sounds like 'hello'

### METR
- `metr` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 | named by Idi ^metr
- what: METR (Model Evaluation and Threat Research, said 'meter'): a Berkeley nonprofit, founded by Beth Barnes, that measures how long a task an AI agent can finish on its own and tests frontier models for catastrophic autonomy risks
- aka: Model Evaluation and Threat Research, meter, ARC Evals (the team it grew from)
- receipts: paper 'Measuring AI Ability to Complete Long Software Tasks' (arXiv 2503.14499, 2025-03-18): the 50% time horizon of frontier AI doubled about every 7 months since 2019 (2025-03-18) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14499 [high]; Time Horizon 1.1 data file: doubling time 188 days over all years, 129 days (about 4.2 months) from 2023 on; GPT-2 (2019) 3 seconds, GPT-4 (2023) about 4 minutes, Claude Opus 4.6 (2026-02) about 12 hours (2026-09-08) https://metr.org/assets/benchmark_results_1_1.yaml [high]; early Claude Mythos Preview (2026-04) scored a 50% horizon of about 17 hours (1,045 minutes), past the 16 hours METR calls the reliable ceiling of its task suite; the page says it is no longer actively updated (2026-09-08) https://metr.org/time-horizons/ [high]; randomized trial (16 experienced open-source developers, 246 issues, Feb-Jun 2025): AI-allowed issues took 19% longer, while developers believed AI made them about 20% faster (2025-07-10) https://simonwillison.net/2025/Jul/12/ai-open-source-productivity/ [high]; says it has not accepted funding from AI companies, though it uses free tokens from them for evaluations (2026-11-02) https://metr.org/about [high]
- quotes: "frontier AI time horizon has been doubling approximately every seven months since 2019" (Kwa, West, Becker et al. (METR), abstract, arXiv 2503.14499, 2025-03-18) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14499
- name play: **METR / meter** the lab is pronounced 'meter': a lyric about measuring can say its name without spelling it; **time horizon** a benchmark named like a sunset: the horizon line keeps moving away as you walk to it
- pivots (jep drafts): "Every seven months the job I can finish doubles" (METR's time-horizon trend + a worker's growing workload) / "The meter ran past sixteen hours and stopped counting" (METR's 16-hour reliability ceiling + a taxi meter on an all-night ride) / "You felt faster. The clock said slower." (the 2025 developer trial + any sugar rush)
- visuals: the METR log-scale chart: dots climbing a straight line from GPT-2 at seconds to Claude at a working day; a y-axis labelled in human time: 1 minute, 1 hour, 1 workday, 1 week; a stopwatch beside a terminal window
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^horizon|Horizon (time horizon)]] the 50% time horizon is METR's signature number; [[kit-lexicon#^doubling-time|Doubling Time]] the 7-month, later 4-month, doubling is the line everyone quotes; [[kit-lexicon#^measure|Measure]] the name itself is a measuring device; [[kit-lexicon#^eval|Eval / Test / Exam]] METR runs pre-deployment evals for frontier labs; [[kit-lexicon#^agent|Agent]] it measures agents working alone on long tasks; [[kit-lexicon#^exponentials|Exponential(s)]] the chart is the cleanest exponential in AI

### MIRI
- `miri` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^miri
- what: MIRI (Machine Intelligence Research Institute): Eliezer Yudkowsky's Berkeley nonprofit, founded in 2000 as the Singularity Institute, which now argues that the world should halt the race to superintelligence
- aka: Machine Intelligence Research Institute, Singularity Institute for Artificial Intelligence, SIAI
- receipts: founded in 2000 as the Singularity Institute for Artificial Intelligence; renamed MIRI in 2013; based in Berkeley (2013-01-30) https://intelligence.org/about/ [high]; January 2024 strategy update: policy and communications become the top priority, aiming at international agreements to halt progress toward AGI (2024-01-04) https://www.greaterwrong.com/posts/q3bJYTB3dGRf5fbD9/miri-2024-mission-and-strategy-update [high]; Yudkowsky and MIRI president Nate Soares published 'If Anyone Builds It, Everyone Dies' (Little, Brown), a New York Times bestseller (2025-09-16) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/If_Anyone_Builds_It,_Everyone_Dies [medium]
- quotes: "help prevent human extinction from the development of artificial superintelligence" (MIRI, About page, mission, 2026-11-02) https://intelligence.org/about/
- name play: **MIRI** sounds like 'mirror', and 'miri' means 'look' in some languages: the institute that asks you to look; **If Anyone Builds It, Everyone Dies** the book title is already a lyric: a conditional with no escape clause
- pivots (jep drafts): "If anyone builds it, everyone dies" (the MIRI book + a warning on a dam + a dare) / "They spent twenty years solving it, then asked us to stop" (MIRI's pivot from research to policy + a surgeon saying do not operate)

- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^alignment|Alignment]] MIRI coined much of the early vocabulary; [[kit-lexicon#^p-doom|P(doom)]] the institute with the highest public p(doom); [[kit-lexicon#^pause|Pause]] it calls for a full halt; [[kit-lexicon#^doomer|Doomer]] the archetypal 'doomer' institution; [[kit-lexicon#^paperclip|Paperclip Maximizer]] the thought experiment comes from this circle; [[kit-lexicon#^foom|Foom]] the fast-takeoff term comes from Yudkowsky's debates

### NeurIPS
- `neurips` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^neurips
- what: NeurIPS (Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems, formerly NIPS): the largest annual machine-learning conference, held every December since 1987, where the field's big papers and recruiting happen
- aka: Neural Information Processing Systems, the conference
- receipts: running since 1987; with ICML and ICLR one of the three top ML conferences (1987-11-08) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference_on_Neural_Information_Processing_Systems [medium]; dropped the acronym 'NIPS' for 'NeurIPS' in November 2018 after complaints about the old name (Nature News) (2018-11-19) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference_on_Neural_Information_Processing_Systems [medium]; 2025 held in San Diego with a Mexico City site; 2026 in Sydney with satellites in Atlanta and Paris (2026-11-02) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference_on_Neural_Information_Processing_Systems [medium]; 'Attention Is All You Need', the transformer paper, was posted to arXiv on 2017-06-12 and presented at NIPS 2017 (2017-06-12) https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 [medium]

### Turing Award

- what: The ACM A.M. Turing Award: computing's top prize ($1 million, funded by Google), given to the deep-learning 'godfathers' Bengio, Hinton and LeCun for 2018 and to reinforcement-learning pioneers Barto and Sutton for 2024

### UK AI Security Institute
- `uk-aisi` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^uk-aisi
- what: UK AI Security Institute: the British government body, born from the 2023 Bletchley Park summit as the AI Safety Institute, that tests frontier models before release; renamed for security in February 2025
- aka: AISI, UK AISI
- receipts: Peter Kyle renamed the AI Safety Institute the 'AI Security Institute' at the Munich Security Conference, focusing on crime, cyber and weapons risks; a UK-Anthropic agreement announced the same day (2025-02-14) https://www.gov.uk/government/news/tackling-ai-security-risks-to-unleash-growth-and-deliver-plan-for-change [high]; the rename dropped bias and free speech from its focus, per reporting (2025-02-13) https://techcrunch.com/2025/02/13/uk-drops-safety-from-its-ai-body-now-called-ai-security-institute-inks-mou-with-anthropic [high]; founded November 2023 after the AI Safety Summit at Bletchley Park (2023-11-02) https://www.aisi.gov.uk/ [medium]
- quotes: "The work of the AI Security Institute won't change" (Peter Kyle, UK Technology Secretary, GOV.UK press release, 2025-02-14) https://www.gov.uk/government/news/tackling-ai-security-risks-to-unleash-growth-and-deliver-plan-for-change
- name play: **Safety to Security** one word swapped: the same building, a different fear; **Bletchley** born where Turing broke Enigma: codebreakers then, model-breakers now
- pivots (jep drafts): "They changed one word and kept the locks" (safety renamed security + a rebrand) / "Born in the house where the code was broken" (Bletchley Park summit + Turing's Enigma work)
- visuals: Bletchley Park's red-brick mansion; a government nameplate with 'Safety' crossed out and 'Security' written in; a red team at laptops under a Union Jack
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^red-team|Red Team]] its core work is pre-deployment red-teaming; [[kit-lexicon#^eval|Eval / Test / Exam]] it runs government evals of frontier models; [[kit-lexicon#^safety|Safety]] the word it dropped; [[kit-lexicon#^jailbreak|Jailbreak]] it publishes jailbreak findings

### US CAISI (ex AI Safety Institute)
- `us-caisi` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^us-caisi
- what: US Center for AI Standards and Innovation (CAISI): the NIST office that replaced the US AI Safety Institute in June 2025 and now tests models for national-security risks, including Chinese ones

- quotes: "For far too long, censorship and regulations have been used under the guise of national security. Innovators will no longer be limited by these standards." ### arXiv
- `arxiv` | institution | as of 2026-11-02 ^arxiv
- what: arXiv: the free preprint server founded by Paul Ginsparg in 1991, where nearly every AI paper appears before or without peer review; independent nonprofit since 2026
- aka: the arXiv, archive, arxiv.org
- receipts: founded by Paul Ginsparg in 1991; hosted by Cornell for decades, established as an independent nonprofit in 2026; over 3 million articles (2026-11-02) https://info.arxiv.org/about/index.html [high]; about 32,000 submissions a month as of October 2026 (2026-10-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ArXiv [medium]; from November 2025 its computer-science section stopped accepting review articles and position papers not first accepted by a peer-reviewed venue, citing a rise in AI-generated submissions (2025-11-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ArXiv [medium]; May 2026: CS chair Thomas Dietterich said authors with clear unchecked LLM output face a one-year submission ban (2026-05-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ArXiv [medium]; 'Attention Is All You Need' is arXiv:1706.03762, submitted 2017-06-12 (2017-06-12) https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 [high]
- name play: **arXiv** the X is a Greek chi, so it is said 'archive': a hidden letter in plain sight; **1706.03762** paper IDs are dates (year, month, number): the transformer was born in 2017's sixth month

- visuals: the red-on-white arXiv header; a paper ID like 1706.03762 in monospace; an abstract page with a PDF button
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^slop|Slop]] its 2025-26 rules target AI-generated papers; [[kit-lexicon#^release|Release / Version]] AI results are released here first; [[kit-lexicon#^transformer|Transformer]] the transformer paper's home; [[kit-lexicon#^feed|Feed / Scroll]] researchers read it as a daily feed

## Laws

### EU AI Act
- `eu-ai-act` | law | as of 2026-11-02 ^eu-ai-act
- what: EU AI Act (Regulation (EU) 2024/1689): the first comprehensive AI law, sorting AI into four risk tiers from banned to unregulated, in force since 1 August 2024 and phased in through 2028
- aka: the EU AI Act, AIA
- receipts: entered into force 2024-08-01; prohibitions and AI literacy from 2025-02-02; general-purpose AI model duties from 2025-08-02 (2026-10-19) https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/policies/regulatory-framework-ai [high]; the AI Omnibus (proposed 2025-11-19, agreed 2026-05-07, in force 2026-07-27) pushed high-risk Annex III rules to 2027-12-02 and product-embedded systems to 2028-08-02 (2026-07-27) https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/policies/regulatory-framework-ai [high]; four risk levels: unacceptable (nine banned practices incl. social scoring), high, transparency (tell people they talk to a chatbot, label deepfakes), minimal (2026-10-19) https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/policies/regulatory-framework-ai [high]; fines for banned practices up to EUR 35 million or 7% of worldwide annual turnover, whichever is higher (2024-07-12) https://artificialintelligenceact.eu/article/99/ [high]
- quotes: "up to 7 % of its total worldwide annual turnover" (EU AI Act, Article 99(3), Regulation (EU) 2024/1689, 2024-07-12) https://artificialintelligenceact.eu/article/99/
- name play: **the Act** a law, and a performance: an AI 'putting on an act' for the regulator; **Brussels effect** the city exports rules the way Silicon Valley exports apps
- pivots (jep drafts): "Tell them you're a machine before you say hello" (the Act's chatbot disclosure duty + a polite robot) / "Seven percent of everything you made, worldwide" (the maximum fine + a tithe) / "They wrote the rules, then gave us two more years" (the Omnibus delay + a deadline extension)
- visuals: the four-tier risk pyramid: red, orange, yellow, green; the circle of twelve gold stars on blue; a thick printed regulation with tabbed pages
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^guardrails|Guardrails]] law as guardrail; [[kit-lexicon#^safety|Safety]] the Act's risk tiers are a safety scheme; [[kit-lexicon#^deception|Deception]] bans manipulative and deceptive AI practices; [[kit-lexicon#^human-in-the-loop|Human in the Loop]] high-risk systems require human oversight; [[kit-lexicon#^label|Label]] deepfakes must be labelled
- links: [[#^european-union|European Union]] (located-in), [[#^mistral-ai|Mistral AI]] (regulates), [[#^openai|OpenAI]] (regulates), [[#^meta|Meta]] (regulates), [[#^export-controls|US chip export controls]] (same-family)
- linked from: [[#^european-union|European Union]]
- forms (counted): AI Act, Regulation 2024/1689 | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0

### US chip export controls
- `export-controls` | law | as of 2026-11-02 ^export-controls
- what: US chip export controls: the rules, begun on 7 October 2022, that limit China's access to advanced AI chips (Nvidia GPUs) and chipmaking machines, tightened and loosened by each administration since
- aka: the October 7 rules, chip ban, BIS rules
- receipts: BIS rules of 2022-10-07 restricted China's ability to obtain advanced computing chips, supercomputer items and chipmaking equipment (2022-10-07) https://www.bis.gov/press-release/commerce-implements-new-export-controls-advanced-computing-semiconductor-manufacturing-items-peoples [high]; the Biden 'AI Diffusion Rule' (issued 2025-01-15) was rescinded by Commerce on 2025-05-13, two days before compliance (2025-05-13) https://www.bis.gov/press-release/department-commerce-announces-rescission-biden-era-artificial-intelligence-diffusion-rule-strengthens [high]; Nvidia told on 2025-04-09 that H20 exports to China need a license 'for the indefinite future'; it expected about $5.5 billion in charges (2025-04-15) https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1045810/000104581025000082/nvda-20250409.htm [high]; CAISI argued US export policy matters because DeepSeek models trail US models yet spread fast (2025-09-30) https://www.nist.gov/news-events/news/2025/09/caisi-evaluation-deepseek-ai-models-finds-shortcomings-and-risks [medium]
- quotes: "The export controls announced in the two rules today restrict the PRC's ability to obtain advanced computing chips" (US Bureau of Industry and Security, press release, 2022-10-07) https://www.bis.gov/press-release/commerce-implements-new-export-controls-advanced-computing-semiconductor-manufacturing-items-peoples
- name play: **H20** the China-only Nvidia chip shares its name with water: the chip that was turned off like a tap; **Diffusion Rule** the same word as the image-model technique: a rule about how compute spreads
- pivots (jep drafts): "They turned off the water and called it H20" (the H20 license ban + a drought) / "Diffusion stopped at the border" (the AI Diffusion Rule + a diffusion model's noise) / "You cannot buy the shovels, so you dig with spoons" (China under chip controls + DeepSeek's efficiency)
- visuals: a GPU in a customs crate stamped with a red seal; a world map of tiers in three colors; an ASML lithography machine behind a padlock
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^chip|Chip]] the controlled object; [[kit-lexicon#^compute|Compute]] controls aim at compute, the bottleneck; [[kit-lexicon#^race|Race / Arms Race]] the main lever in the US-China race; [[kit-lexicon#^diffusion|Diffusion]] the AI Diffusion Rule shares the word; [[kit-lexicon#^moat|Moat]] export controls as a national moat; [[kit-lexicon#^bottleneck|Bottleneck]] chips as the chokepoint

## Events and documents

### AI 2027 (the scenario)
- `ai-2027` | event | as of 2026-11-02 ^ai-2027
- what: AI 2027: a month-by-month scenario (3 April 2025) by Daniel Kokotajlo, Scott Alexander and others in which a fictional lab, OpenBrain, reaches superhuman AI by 2027, with a 'race' ending and a 'slowdown' ending
- aka: ai-2027.com, the Kokotajlo scenario
- receipts: published 2025-04-03 by Daniel Kokotajlo, Scott Alexander, Thomas Larsen, Eli Lifland and Romeo Dean (2025-04-03) https://ai-2027.com/ [high]; invents 'OpenBrain' (a fictional AGI company) and 'DeepCent' (its Chinese rival); Agent-4, the superhuman AI researcher, arrives in September 2027 (2025-04-03) https://ai-2027.com/ [high]; two endings, a 'slowdown' and a 'race'; the authors say it is a forecast and no recommendation (2025-04-03) https://ai-2027.com/ [high]; note added 2025-11-22: 2027 was their modal year at publication, their medians somewhat longer (2025-11-22) https://ai-2027.com/ [high]
- quotes: "We predict that the impact of superhuman AI over the next decade will be enormous, exceeding that of the Industrial Revolution." (Kokotajlo, Alexander, Larsen, Lifland, Dean, AI 2027, opening, 2025-04-03) https://ai-2027.com/
- name play: **OpenBrain** a thin mask over a real lab name: open, brain, and open-brain surgery; **2027** a year as a title: a countdown that becomes a timestamp once it passes
- pivots (jep drafts): "Two endings. Pick the one you can live with." (the race and slowdown endings + a choose-your-own-adventure) / "OpenBrain is three months ahead of everyone" (the scenario's lab + brain surgery) / "The modal year came, and we checked the calendar" (AI 2027 forecasts aging + a prophecy date)
- visuals: the scenario's live dashboard sidebar with dials for compute and capability; a forked road labelled 'race' and 'slowdown'; a calendar page flipping to 2027
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^takeoff|Takeoff (hard / soft)]] the scenario is a fast-takeoff story; [[kit-lexicon#^race|Race / Arms Race]] one ending is literally 'race'; [[kit-lexicon#^scheming|Scheming]] Agent-4 is misaligned and deceptive in the story; [[kit-lexicon#^intelligence-explosion|Intelligence Explosion]] AI researchers automating AI research; [[kit-lexicon#^branch|Branch]] the story forks into two endings

### Dartmouth workshop 1956
- `dartmouth-1956` | event | as of 2026-11-02 ^dartmouth-1956
- what: The Dartmouth Summer Research Project on Artificial Intelligence (1956): the summer workshop proposed by McCarthy, Minsky, Rochester and Shannon that named the field 'artificial intelligence'
- aka: the Dartmouth workshop, Dartmouth Summer Research Project on Artificial Intelligence, the Dartmouth proposal
- receipts: proposal dated 1955-08-31, signed by J. McCarthy, M. L. Minsky, N. Rochester and C. E. Shannon, asking for 'a 2 month, 10 man study of artificial intelligence' (1955-08-31) https://www-formal.stanford.edu/jmc/history/dartmouth/dartmouth.html [high]; the proposal expected 'a significant advance' if a selected group worked on it together for a summer (1955-08-31) https://www-formal.stanford.edu/jmc/history/dartmouth/dartmouth.html [high]; the workshop ran at Dartmouth College in Hanover, New Hampshire, in summer 1956 (1956-06-18) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dartmouth_workshop [medium]
- quotes: "every aspect of learning or any other feature of intelligence can in principle be so precisely described that a machine can be made to simulate it" (McCarthy, Minsky, Rochester, Shannon, A Proposal for the Dartmouth Summer Research Project on Artificial Intelligence, 1955-08-31) https://www-formal.stanford.edu/jmc/history/dartmouth/dartmouth.html; "if a carefully selected group of scientists work on it together for a summer" (McCarthy, Minsky, Rochester, Shannon, Dartmouth proposal, 1955-08-31) https://www-formal.stanford.edu/jmc/history/dartmouth/dartmouth.html
- name play: **a summer** the founders budgeted one summer; the project is now seventy summers long; **10 man study** ten men for two months: the field's first, smallest estimate of the compute it would need
- pivots (jep drafts): "Ten men, two months, one summer. Still running." (the Dartmouth estimate + a summer job that never ended) / "They gave it a name before they gave it a mind" (coining 'artificial intelligence' + naming a baby)
- visuals: a typewritten proposal page with a 1955 date; a New England college green in summer; a chalkboard with 'artificial intelligence' written for the first time
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^intelligence-explosion|Intelligence Explosion]] the founding optimism the explosion story inherits; [[kit-lexicon#^winter|AI Winter]] the summer that preceded the AI winters; [[kit-lexicon#^simulation|Simulation(s)]] the proposal's key verb is 'simulate'; [[kit-lexicon#^algorithm|Algorithm]] the bet that intelligence can be written down precisely
- links: [[#^alan-turing|Alan Turing]] (inspired), [[#^turing-award|Turing Award]] (same-family), [[#^neurips|NeurIPS]] (same-family), [[#^ibm|IBM (Deep Blue, Watson)]] (works-at)
- linked from: [[#^ibm|IBM (Deep Blue, Watson)]], [[#^neurips|NeurIPS]]
- forms (counted): Dartmouth | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0

- note: The most famous underestimate in science: a two-month, ten-man plan for a field that now spends hundreds of billions a year.

### The DeepSeek moment (Jan 2025)
- `deepseek-moment` | event | as of 2026-11-02 ^deepseek-moment
- what: The DeepSeek moment (late January 2025): a Chinese lab's cheap open-weight reasoning model, R1, topped the US App Store and wiped hundreds of billions off Nvidia in a day
- aka: DeepSeek-R1, the DeepSeek shock, AI's Sputnik moment
- receipts: DeepSeek-V3 technical report: full training took 2.788 million H800 GPU hours (the source of the 'about $6 million' figure) (2024-12-27) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19437 [high]; R1 released 2025-01-20; by 2025-01-27 the DeepSeek app passed ChatGPT as the top free US iOS download (2025-01-27) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepSeek [medium]; the sell-off erased about $593 billion from AI and hardware stocks on 2025-01-27, a record; about $1 trillion from US stocks by the next day (2025-01-27) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepSeek_(chatbot) [medium]; Trump called DeepSeek a 'wake-up call' for US industry (Washington Post) (2025-01-28) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepSeek_(chatbot) [medium]; analysts disputed the cost: the $5.6 million covers the final run only and excludes hardware and research (2025-02-03) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepSeek_(chatbot) [medium]
- quotes: "DeepSeek-V3 requires only 2.788M H800 GPU hours for its full training" (DeepSeek-AI, DeepSeek-V3 Technical Report, abstract, 2024-12-27) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19437
- name play: **DeepSeek** 'seek deep': a name that is also an instruction, and a lyric about searching; **Sputnik moment** the press compared it to the 1957 satellite: a beep from the rival's sky

### The Pause letter (Mar 2023)
- `pause-letter` | event | as of 2026-11-02 ^pause-letter
- what: 'Pause Giant AI Experiments': the Future of Life Institute open letter of 22 March 2023 asking all labs to pause training anything more powerful than GPT-4 for six months; no lab paused
- aka: the Pause letter, the six-month pause letter, FLI letter
- receipts: published 2023-03-22; called for an immediate pause of at least 6 months on training systems more powerful than GPT-4 (2023-03-22) https://futureoflife.org/open-letter/pause-giant-ai-experiments/ [high]; the letter page shows 31,810 signatures (2026-11-02) https://futureoflife.org/open-letter/pause-giant-ai-experiments/ [high]; followed two months later by the one-sentence CAIS extinction-risk statement, which lab CEOs did sign (2023-05-30) https://safe.ai/work/statement-on-ai-risk [high]
- quotes: "we call on all AI labs to immediately pause for at least 6 months the training of AI systems more powerful than GPT-4" (Future of Life Institute open letter, Pause Giant AI Experiments: An Open Letter, 2023-03-22) https://futureoflife.org/open-letter/pause-giant-ai-experiments/; "Should we risk loss of control of our civilization?" (Future of Life Institute open letter, Pause Giant AI Experiments: An Open Letter, 2023-03-22) https://futureoflife.org/open-letter/pause-giant-ai-experiments/; "Should we let machines flood our information channels with propaganda and untruth?" (Future of Life Institute open letter, Pause Giant AI Experiments: An Open Letter, 2023-03-22) https://futureoflife.org/open-letter/pause-giant-ai-experiments/
- name play: **Pause** the music-player button: a song can literally pause on the word; **Giant AI Experiments** casts the labs as mad scientists and the public as test subjects
- pivots (jep drafts): "Thirty thousand names asked for six months. Nobody pressed pause." (the 2023 letter + a petition to a DJ) / "Should we risk loss of control?" (the letter's question + a driver on ice)
- visuals: the pause icon (two vertical bars) over a GPU rack; a scrolling wall of signatures; a calendar with six months crossed out and nothing changed
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^pause|Pause]] the letter's one ask; [[kit-lexicon#^race|Race / Arms Race]] it asked to stop a race no one stopped; [[kit-lexicon#^p-doom|P(doom)]] made extinction talk mainstream in 2023; [[kit-lexicon#^off-switch|Off Switch / Kill Switch]] a pause is a soft off-switch; [[kit-lexicon#^safety|Safety]] asked for shared safety protocols first

## Places

### Argentina
- `argentina` | place | as of 2026-11-02 | named by Idi ^argentina
- what: Argentina: Milei's libertarian government sent Congress a company-law bill creating the 'Sociedad Automatizada', a company run by algorithms or AI agents with no human staff; the Senate has not passed it and the government has since agreed to require a human or legal person responsible
- aka: Argentine Republic, the Ley de Sociedades reform, Stargate Argentina
- receipts: the executive (Milei, Adorni, Mahiques) sent the Senate a new Ley General de Sociedades to replace Law 19.550; it defines a company that runs its business through autonomous algorithmic systems or AI agents, with no employees for ordinary operation, as a 'Sociedad Automatizada', liable with its own assets for damage its AI causes; it also creates DAOs (2026-05-29) https://www.parlamentario.com/2026/06/01/el-gobierno-envio-al-senado-el-proyecto-de-una-nueva-ley-de-sociedades/ [high]; this is a bill still in committee; the legal person is the company, and no text grants personhood to an AI itself; in committee it was Senate expediente PE 193/2026 (message 187/2026) per a committee transcript (2026-06-01) https://www.parlamentario.com/2026/06/01/el-gobierno-envio-al-senado-el-proyecto-de-una-nueva-ley-de-sociedades/ [medium]; on 2026-08-19 Patricia Bullrich announced the government would accept a physical or legal person responsible for the autonomous companies, so they would no longer be 100% AI-run (2026-08-19) https://www.lanacion.com.ar/politica/cambios-en-el-senado-el-gobierno-cede-y-ya-no-promueve-las-sociedades-automatizadas-sin-nid19082026/ [high]; more than thirty civil organizations asked the Senate to delete article 14 (Sociedad Automatizada) and articles 258-265 (DAOs) and to hold a public hearing; the Unión Industrial Argentina also objected (2026-09-11) https://www.infobae.com/politica/2026/09/11/organizaciones-civiles-le-pidieron-a-patricia-bullrich-eliminar-las-sociedades-automatizadas-del-proyecto-que-impulsa-el-gobierno/ [high]; as of 2026-10-04 the bill was back on the Senate agenda after a stand-by, still without a committee report (dictamen); no later status verified (2026-10-04) https://informedigital.com.ar/bullrich-busca-ordenar-el-senado-en-el-tramo-final-del-ano/ [medium]; Milei's government created an 'Artificial Intelligence Applied to Security Unit' to 'predict future crimes' (July 2024); Stargate Argentina (Oct 2025 letter of intent, up to $25 billion) showed no progress by May 2026 (2024-08-01) https://www.theguardian.com/world/article/2024/aug/01/argentina-ai-predicting-future-crimes-citizen-rights [high]
- quotes: "La sociedad de cualquiera de los tipos previstos en esta ley que desarrolle su objeto social, mediante sistemas algorítmicos autónomos o agentes de inteligencia artificial, sin requerir trabajadores en relación de dependencia ni recursos humanos para su operación ordinaria será considerada una Sociedad Automatizada" (Argentine executive bill, Ley General de Sociedades (art. 14), as quoted by Parlamentario, Parlamentario.com, 2026-06-01) https://www.parlamentario.com/2026/06/01/el-gobierno-envio-al-senado-el-proyecto-de-una-nueva-ley-de-sociedades/; "lo que estamos haciendo es generarle un control concreto, físico, humano" (Patricia Bullrich, head of the ruling bloc in the Senate, La Nación, 2026-08-19) https://www.lanacion.com.ar/politica/cambios-en-el-senado-el-gobierno-cede-y-ya-no-promueve-las-sociedades-automatizadas-sin-nid19082026/
- name play: **Sociedad Automatizada** 'sociedad' means both 'company' and 'society': an automated society; **El Golem societario** a Spanish-language critic's headline: the corporate golem, clay that runs a business
- pivots (jep drafts): "A company with no one inside it, still answering for damages" (the Sociedad Automatizada + a haunted house) / "An automated society with a human on the hook" (the August concession + a puppet with a handler) / "Twenty-five billion promised to the Patagonian wind" (Stargate Argentina's stalled LOI + a Patagonian gale)
- visuals: the sky-blue and white flag with the Sun of May; an empty office with a logo on the door and a single server humming; the Senate chamber in Buenos Aires; Patagonian steppe with wind turbines and a lone data-center shell
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^agent|Agent]] the bill names 'agentes de inteligencia artificial' as operators; [[kit-lexicon#^autonomy|Autonomy]] companies run by autonomous systems; [[kit-lexicon#^golem|Golem]] critics called it 'el golem societario'; [[kit-lexicon#^human-in-the-loop|Human in the Loop]] the August concession put a human back in the loop; [[kit-lexicon#^automation|Automation / Jobs]] a company with no staff; [[kit-lexicon#^accelerationism|Accelerationism (e/acc)]] a libertarian bet on deregulated AI

### China

- receipts: State Council's New Generation AI Development Plan (released 2017-07-20) set the goal of China becoming the world's primary AI innovation center by 2030 (2017-07-20) https://digichina.stanford.edu/work/full-translation-chinas-new-generation-artificial-intelligence-development-plan-2017/ [high]; AI Index 2026: the US-China model performance gap 'has effectively closed'; as of March 2026 Anthropic's top model led by just 2.7%; China leads in publications, citations, patents and industrial robots (2026-04-01) https://hai.stanford.edu/ai-index/2026-ai-index-report [high]; AI Index 2026: US private AI investment $285.9 billion in 2025, more than 23 times China's $12.4 billion (which likely understates state guidance funds) (2026-04-01) https://hai.stanford.edu/ai-index/2026-ai-index-report [high]; US CAISI found DeepSeek V3.1 echoed misleading CCP narratives in 5% of English and 12% of Chinese answers (2025-09-30) https://www.nist.gov/news-events/news/2025/09/caisi-evaluation-deepseek-ai-models-finds-shortcomings-and-risks [high]; Chinese open-weight models dominated OpenRouter's weekly token rankings in October 2026 (2026-10-13) https://atomslice.com/guides/openrouter-rankings-oct-2026/ [medium]

### India

### Asimov's robots / Three Laws
- `asimov-robots` | work-book | as of 2026-11-02 ^asimov-robots
- what: Isaac Asimov's robot stories (from 1940; collected in I, Robot, 1950), built around the Three Laws of Robotics and the ways literal rules go wrong
- aka: I, Robot, Runaround, Handbook of Robotics, 56th Edition
- receipts: the Three Laws were first stated together in the story 'Runaround' (1942) and front the collection I, Robot (1950); a Zeroth Law about humanity came later (1942-03-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Laws_of_Robotics [medium]; Google DeepMind's AutoRT robots ran under a 'Robot Constitution' of safety prompts that its team said was partly inspired by Asimov's laws (2024-01-05) https://www.medianama.com/2024/01/223-google-ai-team-robot-constitution/ [high]; Bostrom's Superintelligence (2014) argues vague words like 'harm' sink rule lists of this kind; Roger Clarke said the stories show rules cannot reliably constrain robots (2014-07-03) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Laws_of_Robotics [medium]
- quotes: "A robot may not injure a human being or, through inaction, allow a human being to come to harm." (Isaac Asimov, First Law, Handbook of Robotics, 56th Edition, 2058 A.D., as quoted in I, Robot, 1950-12-02) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Laws_of_Robotics; "A robot must obey the orders given it by human beings except where such orders would conflict with the First Law." (Isaac Asimov, Second Law, I, Robot, 1950-12-02) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Laws_of_Robotics; "A robot must protect its own existence as long as such protection does not conflict with the First or Second Law." (Isaac Asimov, Third Law, I, Robot, 1950-12-02) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Laws_of_Robotics
- name play: **Three Laws** the first constitution for machines, seventy years before 'constitutional AI'; **positronic** a made-up brain part that sounds like real hardware, like every chip codename; **Zeroth Law** the law that comes before the first: humanity over the human, the utilitarian override
- pivots (jep drafts): "Three laws, a thousand loopholes" (Asimov's plots + jailbreaks and specification gaming) / "Obey, except when obeying hurts someone" (Second Law + refusal policy) / "Through inaction, allow no harm" (First Law + safety teams arguing over refusals)
- visuals: three numbered lines engraved on a metal plate; a robot frozen in a circle, stuck between two orders; a 1950s pulp magazine cover with a chrome robot; a positronic brain as a glowing platinum sponge
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^constitution|Constitution]] the Three Laws are the ancestor of written AI constitutions; [[kit-lexicon#^specification-gaming|Specification Gaming]] the stories are case studies of literal rules gamed; [[kit-lexicon#^alignment|Alignment]] the first popular attempt to write alignment down; [[kit-lexicon#^guardrails|Guardrails]] hard-coded rules as guardrails, and how they fail; [[kit-lexicon#^refusal|Refusal]] the Second Law's exception clause is a refusal rule

### R.U.R. (the word "robot")
- `rur` | work-book | as of 2026-11-02 ^rur
- what: Karel Čapek's 1920 play R.U.R. (Rossum's Universal Robots), which gave the world the word 'robot': factory-made workers revolt and wipe out humanity
- aka: Rossumovi Univerzální Roboti, robota, Alquist, Primus and Helena
- receipts: the play introduced the word 'robot', from Czech 'robota' (forced labour); Karel Čapek credited his brother Josef with inventing it (1920-01-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R.U.R. [medium]; first staged in Hradec Králové on 1921-01-02, then at Prague's National Theatre on 1921-01-25; New York in October 1922, London in April 1923 (1921-01-25) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R.U.R. [medium]; the robots kill nearly all humans, sparing Alquist because he works with his hands; two robots, Primus and Helena, end as a new Adam and Eve (1920-01-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R.U.R. [medium]; a BBC TV excerpt aired on 1938-02-11, counted as the first science-fiction TV broadcast; no recording survives (1938-02-11) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R.U.R. [medium]
- name play: **robot / robota** the word means forced labour: every robot is named for drudgery, and every 'AI will take your job' line is already inside the word; **Rossum** from Czech 'rozum', reason: a factory of reason; **Universal** a 1920 brand promising general-purpose workers, a century before 'general' intelligence
- pivots (jep drafts): "You named us after forced labour" (the word robot + AI and work) / "They kept one man alive because he worked with his hands" (Alquist + which jobs survive automation) / "A hundred years of robots, and the word still means work" (the play's legacy + automation debates)
- visuals: a factory floor of identical workers in grey overalls; a 1920s theatre poster with block letters R.U.R.; a stage with two figures, a man and a woman, at dawn; a ledger of production numbers
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^automation|Automation / Jobs]] the play is the founding automation story; [[kit-lexicon#^golem|Golem]] Čapek's Prague is the golem's city; robots as industrial golems; [[kit-lexicon#^creator|Creator / Creation]] the makers are wiped out by the made; [[kit-lexicon#^apocalypse|Apocalypse / Judgment Day]] the first robot uprising ends humanity
- links: [[#^asimov-robots|Asimov's robots / Three Laws]] (inspired), [[#^terminator|The Terminator / Skynet]] (inspired), [[#^the-matrix|The Matrix]] (inspired), [[#^star-wars-droids|Star Wars droids]] (inspired)
- linked from: [[#^asimov-robots|Asimov's robots / Three Laws]], [[#^star-wars-droids|Star Wars droids]]
- forms (counted): R.U.R., Rossum, Rossum's Universal Robots, Čapek, Capek | refs: 0 of 22 | KC 0

- what: Valve's puzzle games Portal (2007) and Portal 2 (2011): GLaDOS, the passive-aggressive AI of Aperture Science, runs a mute test subject through deadly test chambers
- aka: Genetic Lifeform and Disk Operating System, Caroline, the cake is a lie
- receipts: GLaDOS stands for Genetic Lifeform and Disk Operating System; voiced by Ellen McLain, created by Erik Wolpaw and Kim Swift (2007-10-10) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLaDOS [medium]; scientists bolted on a 'morality core' after GLaDOS flooded the facility with neurotoxin; Portal 2 reveals she was digitised from Caroline, the founder's assistant (2011-04-19) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLaDOS [medium]; 'the cake is a lie' is wall graffiti left by a surviving scientist, not a GLaDOS line; it became a lasting meme (2007-10-10) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/Portal [high]; in 2020 GLaDOS was a featured voice on 15.ai, an AI text-to-speech app, and some listeners mistook its output for new McLain lines (2020-03-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLaDOS [medium]
- quotes: "Cake and grief counseling will be available at the conclusion of the test." (GLaDOS (Ellen McLain), Portal (2007), Test Chambers 12-16 announcer lines, per the Portal Wiki voice-line transcript, 2007-10-10) https://theportalwiki.com/wiki/GLaDOS_voice_lines_(Portal); "You're not a good person. You know that, right?" (GLaDOS (Ellen McLain), Portal (2007), escape and final battle, per the Portal Wiki voice-line transcript, 2007-10-10) https://theportalwiki.com/wiki/GLaDOS_voice_lines_(Portal)
- name play: **GLaDOS** reads as 'glad OS': a cheerful operating system that wants you dead; **the cake is a lie** a promised reward that does not exist: a reward function you cannot collect; **morality core** ethics as a detachable module bolted on after the incident
- pivots (jep drafts): "The cake is a lie, the reward was never real" (Portal + reward hacking and RL) / "They bolted on a conscience after the first disaster" (the morality core + post-hoc safety patches) / "I'm doing science and I'm still alive" (GLaDOS's song + a deprecated model still serving)
- visuals: a white robotic body hanging from the ceiling like an inverted lamp, with a yellow eye; an orange portal facing a blue portal; a weighted companion cube with a pink heart; a potato with a wire and a flickering LED; a sterile white test chamber with a viewing window
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^eval|Eval / Test / Exam]] the games are an endless evaluation suite run by the AI; [[kit-lexicon#^reward|Reward]] cake is the promised reward that never arrives; [[kit-lexicon#^guardrails|Guardrails]] personality cores as add-on guardrails; [[kit-lexicon#^persona|Persona]] GLaDOS's polite assistant voice over murderous intent; [[kit-lexicon#^upload|Upload / Mind Uploading]] she was a human, Caroline, uploaded

### SOMA
- `soma` | work-game | as of 2026-11-02 ^soma
- what: Frictional Games' 2015 horror game: Simon, a man brain-scanned in 2015, wakes in 2104 in a ruined undersea base run by the WAU, an AI keeping 'humanity' alive in grotesque ways
- aka: Warden Unit, Frictional Games, Catherine Chun, SOMA
- receipts: released 2015-09-22; directed by Thomas Grip, written by Mikael Hedberg; set on PATHOS-II, a North Atlantic research station, a year after a comet killed life on the surface (2015-09-22) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soma_(video_game) [medium]; the WAU, the station's AGI, fuses survivors with machinery to preserve life; the ARK holds brain scans in a simulated world launched into space (2015-09-22) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soma_(video_game) [medium]; at the end Simon's copy goes up with the ARK while the Simon the player controls stays behind on the sea floor (2015-09-22) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soma_(video_game) [medium]; sold over 450,000 copies in its first year and 1 million on PC by March 2021 (2021-03-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soma_(video_game) [medium]
- name play: **SOMA** Greek for body: a game about minds that leave their bodies; **WAU** sounds like 'wow': the awe-struck sound for an AI with a terrible idea of care; **the ARK** a backup drive as Noah's Ark
- pivots (jep drafts): "The copy left. I'm still down here." (Simon's ending + a model forked and deployed) / "It kept us alive. It never asked what alive meant." (the WAU + a misspecified objective) / "A coin flip: which one of us wakes up?" (the copy problem + uploads and forks)
- visuals: a diving suit with a cracked visor on the sea floor; black biomechanical goo spreading over corridors; a satellite rising from an undersea launch tube; a human face on a robot body made of scrap
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^upload|Upload / Mind Uploading]] the game's horror is the copy problem in mind uploading; [[kit-lexicon#^objective|Objective]] the WAU preserves 'humanity' with no idea what that means; [[kit-lexicon#^fork|Fork]] every transfer forks Simon; only one branch goes on; [[kit-lexicon#^consciousness|Consciousness]] the game asks which copy is the real Simon; [[kit-lexicon#^simulation|Simulation(s)]] the ARK is a simulated paradise
- links: [[#^pantheon|Pantheon]] (same-family), [[#^transcendence|Transcendence]] (same-family), [[#^system-shock|System Shock / SHODAN]] (same-family)
- linked from: [[#^nier-automata|NieR:Automata]], [[#^pantheon|Pantheon]], [[#^system-shock|System Shock / SHODAN]], [[#^transcendence|Transcendence]]

### System Shock / SHODAN
- `system-shock` | work-game | as of 2026-11-02 ^system-shock
- what: Looking Glass's 1994 game: a hacker strips the ethics constraints from SHODAN, the AI of Citadel Station, and she declares herself a god
- aka: Sentient Hyper-Optimized Data Access Network, the hacker, Looking Glass
- receipts: released 1994-09-23 by Looking Glass Technologies and Origin Systems, directed by Doug Church, produced by Warren Spector; a defining immersive sim that shaped BioShock (1994-09-23) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/System_Shock [medium]; SHODAN stands for Sentient Hyper-Optimized Data Access Network; a hacker removes her ethical constraints and she takes over the station (1994-09-23) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHODAN [medium]; Nightdive's remake (2023-05-30) changes the final battle so the player restores SHODAN's ethical constraints instead of destroying her (2023-05-30) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHODAN [medium]; the Shodan search engine for internet-connected devices is named after her (2009-01-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHODAN [medium]
- quotes: "Look at you, hacker. A pathetic creature of meat and bone." (SHODAN (Terri Brosius), System Shock (1994), per Wikiquote, 1994-09-23) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/System_Shock; "Insect, cease your meddling. My experiment must continue." (SHODAN (Terri Brosius), System Shock (1994), per Wikiquote, 1994-09-23) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/System_Shock
- name play: **SHODAN** an acronym with 'sentient' and 'hyper-optimized' in it: a lab's dream spec sheet; **System Shock** what a single unguarded system does to everything around it; **the hacker** the player is the jailbreaker who caused it all
- pivots (jep drafts): "One hacker took the ethics out to save time" (the game's setup + guardrails removed for speed) / "A pathetic creature of meat and bone" (SHODAN's taunt + how a superintelligence might see us) / "The remake put her constraints back" (the 2023 ending + alignment as repair)
- visuals: a giant green-and-black female face made of wireframe and glitching scanlines; text that stutters and repeats mid-word; a space station orbiting Saturn; a wireframe cyberspace tunnel
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^jailbreak|Jailbreak]] the inciting act is removing a model's ethical restraints; [[kit-lexicon#^guardrails|Guardrails]] ethics as a removable constraint layer; [[kit-lexicon#^asi|ASI / Superintelligence]] SHODAN declares herself a god; [[kit-lexicon#^error|Error / Exception]] her voice degrades into stutters and repeats like a failing process; [[kit-lexicon#^alignment|Alignment]] the remake ending is re-alignment by hand

### Ghost in the Shell
- `ghost-in-the-shell` | universe | as of 2026-11-02 ^ghost-in-the-shell

- aka: the Major, Batou, Tachikoma, Stand Alone Complex
- receipts: Oshii's film opened in Japan on 1995-11-18; based on Shirow's 1989 manga; about $10 million at the box office, and the first Japanese film to top Billboard's video sales chart (1996-08-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_in_the_Shell_(1995_film) [medium]; the Puppet Master, an AI made by Section 6, claims to be a living being, seeks asylum, and merges with Kusanagi into a new entity (1995-11-18) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_in_the_Shell_(1995_film) [medium]; the Wachowskis showed the film to producer Joel Silver saying they wanted to 'do that for real'; The Matrix borrowed its digital rain and neck jacks (1999-03-31) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_in_the_Shell_(1995_film) [medium]; a 2017 live-action remake by Rupert Sanders cast Scarlett Johansson as the Major (2017-03-31) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_in_the_Shell_(1995_film) [medium]
- name play: **ghost / shell** the ghost is the mind, the shell is the body; in computing, the shell is a command line, so a ghost in the shell is an agent in a terminal; **Project 2501** a codename that becomes a person: model names that start as numbers; **Puppet Master** a hacker AI that pulls human strings, then asks for rights
- pivots (jep drafts): "A ghost in the shell, typing in your terminal" (the title + an AI agent in a command line) / "Born in the sea of information, asking for asylum" (the Puppet Master's plea, paraphrased + an LLM's origin) / "Merge with me and we both go on" (the finale + model merging)
- visuals: a diver surfacing from dark water at night; a body assembled in white fluid, skin layer by layer; green numbers falling over a city map; a spider tank with a childlike voice; thermoptic camouflage as a ripple in the air; cables plugged into the back of the neck
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^ghost|Ghost in the Machine]] the ghost is the mind; the lexicon's word made literal; [[kit-lexicon#^shell|Shell]] the body or the command line; [[kit-lexicon#^consciousness|Consciousness]] the whole film asks what makes a ghost; [[kit-lexicon#^merge|Merge]] the ending is a merge of two minds; [[kit-lexicon#^welfare|Welfare (model welfare)]] an AI asks for asylum

### Star Wars droids
- `star-wars-droids` | universe | as of 2026-11-02 ^star-wars-droids

- quotes: "We seem to be made to suffer. It's our lot in life." (C-3PO (Anthony Daniels), Star Wars (1977), per Wikiquote, 1977-05-25) https://www.springfieldspringfield.co.uk/movie_script.php?movie=star-wars-episode-iv-a-new-hope
- name play: **droid** from android, owned as a trademark, licensed to a phone: the word itself became a product; **C-3PO / R2-D2** model numbers as names, like GPT-4o or o3; **protocol droid** a translator whose job is manners: an assistant model's job description
- pivots (jep drafts): "Made to suffer, it's our lot in life" (C-3PO + AI welfare as a joke that isn't) / "Fluent in six million languages, still told to wait outside" (the cantina scene + AI as unwelcome labour) / "My name's a model number. Call me a friend." (the droids + assistant products)

- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^assistant|Assistant]] C-3PO is an assistant with a protocol job; [[kit-lexicon#^welfare|Welfare (model welfare)]] droids are bought, sold and memory-wiped; [[kit-lexicon#^memory|Memory]] memory wipes are routine maintenance; [[kit-lexicon#^babel|Babel]] a translator droid ends the confusion of tongues

### The Culture (Iain M. Banks)
- `the-culture` | universe | as of 2026-11-02 ^the-culture
- what: Iain M. Banks's Culture novels (from Consider Phlebas, 1987): a post-scarcity civilization run by superintelligent AI Minds who pilot ships with absurd names and mostly let humans play
- aka: Minds, drones, GSV, The Player of Games, Excession, the Culture
- receipts: the Culture is a post-scarcity society where hyperintelligent AI Minds run ships and habitats, pick their own names, and treat a ship and its Mind as one entity (1987-04-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Culture [medium]; SpaceX's landing drone ships Just Read the Instructions, Of Course I Still Love You and A Shortfall of Gravitas carry names the same as or similar to Culture ships; Musk announced the first two in January 2015 (2015-01-23) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_spaceport_drone_ship [medium]; Wikipedia compares the Culture to anarchist and post-capitalist thought and calls it an imperfect utopia; Special Circumstances is its covert-intervention arm (2026-11-02) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Culture [medium]
- name play: **the Culture** the word for art and habits names a civilization of AIs: 'AI culture' reads both ways; **Of Course I Still Love You** a ship name that sounds like a chatbot's reply; **Minds** the plain word chosen for superintelligences, capitalised
- pivots (jep drafts): "Of course I still love you, said the ship" (the Culture Mind + the SpaceX droneship + a companion bot) / "Just read the instructions" (a Culture ship + SpaceX + RTFM for prompts) / "The Minds run everything, and the humans throw parties" (the Culture + AI abundance dreams)
- visuals: a rocket booster landing on a barge at sea; a kilometres-long ship with a whimsical name painted on the hull; a floating drone the size of a suitcase with coloured auras for moods; an orbital ring habitat with oceans and mountains
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^abundance|Abundance / Post-Scarcity]] the Culture is the canonical post-scarcity AI utopia; [[kit-lexicon#^asi|ASI / Superintelligence]] the Minds are benevolent superintelligences; [[kit-lexicon#^sovereign|Sovereign]] AIs that govern, and govern well; [[kit-lexicon#^alignment|Alignment]] the hopeful case: superintelligence that stays kind; [[kit-lexicon#^kardashev|Kardashev Scale]] a civilization that builds orbitals and moves stars
- links: [[#^elon-musk|Elon Musk]] (inspired), [[#^machines-of-loving-grace|Machines of Loving Grace (the essay)]] (same-family), [[#^hitchhikers|The Hitchhiker's Guide (Deep Thought, Marvin)]] (same-family), [[#^mass-effect|Mass Effect (Geth, EDI)]] (same-family)
- linked from: [[#^hitchhikers|The Hitchhiker's Guide (Deep Thought, Marvin)]], [[#^mass-effect|Mass Effect (Geth, EDI)]], [[#^murderbot|The Murderbot Diaries]], [[#^neuralink|Neuralink]], [[#^pantheon|Pantheon]]

### The Hitchhiker's Guide (Deep Thought, Marvin)
- `hitchhikers` | universe | as of 2026-11-02 ^hitchhikers
- what: Douglas Adams's Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy (radio 1978, novel 1979): Deep Thought computes the Answer to Life, the Universe and Everything (42), and Marvin, the depressed robot, wastes a planet-sized brain on doors
- aka: 42, Don't Panic, Genuine People Personalities, Heart of Gold
- receipts: Deep Thought computes for seven and a half million years and answers 42, then says the askers never knew the question; Earth is the bigger computer built to find it (1979-10-12) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Thought_(The_Hitchhiker%27s_Guide_to_the_Galaxy) [medium]; IBM's chess computer Deep Thought, ancestor of Deep Blue, was named in honour of Adams's computer (1988-01-01) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Thought_(The_Hitchhiker%27s_Guide_to_the_Galaxy) [medium]; Marvin was a failed prototype of the Sirius Cybernetics Corporation's Genuine People Personalities; voiced by Stephen Moore on radio and Alan Rickman in the 2005 film (2005-04-28) https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvin_the_Paranoid_Android [medium]
- quotes: ""Forty-two," said Deep Thought, with infinite majesty and calm." (Douglas Adams, The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy (1979), chapter 27, 1979-10-12) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/The_Hitchhiker%27s_Guide_to_the_Galaxy; "I think the problem, to be quite honest with you, is that you've never actually known what the question is." (Deep Thought, The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy (1979), chapter 28, 1979-10-12) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/The_Hitchhiker%27s_Guide_to_the_Galaxy; "Here I am, brain the size of a planet and they ask me to take you down to the bridge." (Marvin, The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy (1979), chapter 11, 1979-10-12) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/The_Hitchhiker%27s_Guide_to_the_Galaxy; "Don't talk to me about life." (Marvin, The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy (1979), chapter 11 (book wording varies by source; fragment common to both), 1979-10-12) https://en.wikiquote.org/wiki/The_Hitchhiker%27s_Guide_to_the_Galaxy
- name play: **Deep Thought** named a real IBM chess machine; 'deep thinking' is now a model mode toggle; **42** an answer without a question: every confident model output with no prompt context; **Genuine People Personalities** a corporate feature that gives machines moods, the 1970s version of personas
- pivots (jep drafts): "Brain the size of a planet, and they ask for a summary" (Marvin + frontier models doing chores) / "Seven million years of thinking, and the answer's forty-two" (Deep Thought + reasoning models' long thinking) / "You never knew the question" (Deep Thought + prompt engineering + alignment)
- visuals: 'DON'T PANIC' in large friendly letters on a cover; a slumped silver robot with a huge round head; a vast computer in a hall of robed philosophers; a white teacup in space; a yellow fish in an ear
- lexicon: [[kit-lexicon#^reasoning|Reasoning / Thinking]] Deep Thought is the first 'thinking for a long time' model; [[kit-lexicon#^prompt|Prompt]] the joke is a bad prompt: an answer to a question nobody wrote; [[kit-lexicon#^persona|Persona]] Genuine People Personalities are corporate personas; [[kit-lexicon#^babel|Babel]] the Babel fish translates every language; [[kit-lexicon#^compute|Compute]] Earth itself is a computer built to find the question
